**Sovereign Rent and the dialectic of controlled solvency**

In all cases, the host states retain their full Sovereign Rent: they may at any time amend the terms of the leases, increase rents, restrict exports, or limit the Chinese presence. The length of the leases (49 years in Russia, 99 years in Zambia) reduces this risk, but does not eliminate it. The difference compared with Indonesia (2026) is that these states need Chinese investment to modernise their land, which creates a mutual dependency that tempers the temptation to issue a unilateral decree.

Massive Chinese investment could increase the differential rent from these lands: irrigation, fertilisers, infrastructure, improved seeds. It would strengthen China's position in the dialectic of controlled solvency (see §5.5.6) by anchoring the activity in the host territories. However, even with these investments, the operation would not constitute a 'Buyout' as defined by the ToRE: it would remain a productive investment with low externalities, securing supply flows without creating exogenous demand in advance or polarised growth in China.

Legislative changes in the host countries — for example, allowing foreigners to own agricultural land — would not be sufficient to transform these operations into a 'Buyback'. These states would retain their Sovereign Rent, and foreign assets are not 'Spaces of Value' frozen by a symbolic ban; they are fully integrated into the global market, even if Western sanctions restrict access to them.

**Summary: niche accumulation, not a new cycle of Rent Extraction**

The China–Russia partnership, whether involving agricultural land or energy joint ventures (86 projects, $222 billion, key sectors), is a strategic partnership between sovereign states, not a cycle of Rent Extraction. The same applies to all Chinese agricultural investments in Africa, Latin America and South-East Asia. These operations secure physical flows (energy, foodstuffs) and generate shared rents, but they do not resolve China's Stasis.

Foreign land leased or farmed by Chinese interests is an example of **niche accumulation** — a strategy to circumvent domestic land constraints which, whilst not creating a cycle of polarised growth, contributes to the resilience of China's productive apparatus. This niche accumulation is a pragmatic response to the depletion of the stock of domestic value, but it does not constitute a new frontier capable of generating the necessary advance in exogenous demand to restart the cycle of Rent Extraction in China.

#### 5.5.5. Fortified Port Enclaves: a response to sovereignty constraints

Given the vulnerability of processing investments (HPAL plants) to the 'sovereign rent' levied by host states, China has developed an alternative: **Fortified Port Enclaves**. The port of Chancay in Peru, inaugurated in November 2024, is the archetype of this model. Unlike HPAL plants, Chancay is a purely extractive infrastructure: a mineral port designed to export South American copper, lithium and iron ore to China, with no local processing capacity whatsoever.

Chinese loan agreements include structural conditions which, whilst not political conditionalities in the traditional sense, constrain the host state's sovereign room for manoeuvre. Thus, at Chancay: exclusive operating rights granted to COSCO, regulatory exemptions secured through legislation, and an all-assets pledge securing the project's entire assets. This structure makes any unilateral change to the rules by the host state economically destructive.

The '**dialectic of controlled solvency**'^[The concept of the 'dialectic of controlled solvency' is the author's interpretation. It aims to formalise a tension observed between two objectives of Chinese foreign policy — maintaining solvent partners to safeguard investment outlets and returns, whilst managing the risks of devaluation associated with the Sovereign Rent. It does not correspond to any official Chinese doctrine, nor is it formulated as such in institutional documents or public statements. This interpretation is offered as a possible reading, not as a description of Beijing's intended strategy. The logic underpinning this strategy rests on the idea that the host state is kept afloat — just enough to service its debt and purchase Chinese goods — but not enough to develop an autonomous industry or break free from dependence. China wishes neither to render its partners completely insolvent (as they would cease to be markets) nor to make them fully solvent (as they would break free from their dependence on China). The aim is a precarious balance, calibrated by contractual clauses, collateral and regulatory exemptions.]

Chancay illustrates the temporal duality of the Fortified Enclaves: a defensive transition phase (geopolitical turnstile) that shifts into a stationary offensive phase (automated robotic hub). This model applies to all strategic ports financed by the Silk Roads: Djibouti, Gwadar, Piraeus, Hambantota. China is abandoning industrial transformation to focus on securing physical flows.

#### 5.5.7. The accounting role of the intangible bubble

The bubble in AI tokens and carbon credits (see Part III) plays an accounting role in this mechanism. It allows for the nominal revaluation of the collateral for Chinese loans (the rights to exploit foreign assets) without this revaluation corresponding to an increase in the physical value of the assets. The destruction of material wealth is thus partially 'absorbed' by the inflation of Chinese intangible assets. But this mechanism has its limits: it cannot indefinitely offset massive real losses, as the Venezuelan experience demonstrates.

**Concluding remark:** This externalisation mechanism is not a strategy of domination but a structural response to the exhaustion of China's material cycle. China, like any state that has reached the Nodal Point, must manage the destruction of its material wealth. What is specific to China is that it has attempted to offload part of this destruction onto the host states of its foreign investments. This attempt has seen partial successes (port enclaves) and costly failures (Indonesia, Venezuela). The overhaul of China's overseas lending instruments — notably through the widespread adoption of 'Fortified Enclaves' — aims to stabilise this mechanism and reduce the risks of conflict linked to 'sovereign rent', without ever eliminating them entirely. Through successive crises, China is learning that structural asymmetry does not confer absolute immunity, and that managing the periphery requires a delicate balance between securing cash flows and accepting occasional losses.

---

## Chapter 6 – The Impasse of the Intangible

### Introduction: The epistemological shift and the limits of analogy

The two preceding sections have demonstrated, by adhering strictly to its ontological foundations, that China's cycle of material accumulation conformed to the laws of the Theory of Rent Extraction (ToRE) right up to its conclusion: the Nodal Point. The depletion of the stock of readily mobilisable land and geographical value necessitated a structural shift.

Faced with this constraint, the Chinese authorities have embarked on a 'Third Transformation' by attempting to create new forms of scarcity in the spheres of carbon, biological data and artificial intelligence. However, before analysing the mechanisms of this mutation, a rigorous epistemological clarification is required: the Third Mutation does not, strictly speaking, constitute a new cycle of Rent Extraction.

The Theory of Rent Extraction (ToRE) and the Structural Geography that underpins it postulate that Rent Extraction operates on assets kept off the market by symbolic foundations, collective beliefs or foundational anthropological taboos (sacred land, the seigneurial domain, the *Hukou*). Atmospheric carbon, genomic data or computing tokens do not meet these criteria: they are unvalued externalities or creations *ex nihilo* established by regulatory decree. Consequently, to speak of the 'Rent Extraction of the Intangible' or the 'AI Nodal Point' would be a theoretical misinterpretation that would strip the ToRE of its specificity.

The value of the analysis lies elsewhere: in the fact that the CCP State has devised regulatory mechanisms that faithfully replicate the operational mechanics of Rent Extraction, whilst freeing itself from its fundamental nature. We shall refer to this phenomenon as **structural isomorphism**.

Although this does not involve the transfer of a pre-existing asset, the CCP State artificially creates scarcity (quotas, access standards), mobilises advance credit to purchase it, generates exogenous demand in advance, and organises primary, secondary and tertiary markets that capture differential rents. The sequence is identical, but the underlying asset is a purely normative construct (a state rent) rather than a frozen historical value.

Similarly, whilst we may currently observe underutilised data centres in western China or a collapse in Western valuations, this does not represent the attainment of a 'Nodal Point' in Rebour's sense (which requires the depletion of a finite stock), but rather what we shall call a **Breakpoint in the Sterile Loop**: a crisis of normative overcapacity in which injected credit can no longer generate the expected increasing returns, whilst nevertheless reproducing morphological manifestations (unpaid debt, empty infrastructure, capital flight) identical to those of the material Nodal Point.

Finally, whilst the economic ToRE does not formally distinguish between material and immaterial wealth, Structural Geography is, for its part, fundamentally a theory of spatial form. Analysis of this Third Mutation reveals its Achilles' heel: the lack of morphological depth. The algorithm does not generate urban form; it requires neither concrete nor mass logistics. It creates market value without creating the physical infrastructure necessary for the spatial distribution of rent towards mass employment.

This section is therefore structured as an extension by analogy. It examines how China has attempted to simulate a cycle of Rent Extraction in the immaterial realm through an '**Immaterial Trimorphism**' (Carbon, Life, Cognitive Data), why this simulation reached its own internal limits (algorithmic stasis), how it was forced to resort to aggressive outsourcing of its overcapacity (the strategy of external encirclement), and why, ultimately, the immaterial can provide only palliative buffers and not a substitute driving force.

### Immaterial Trimorphism — An Analogue Extension of the ToRE

This chapter explores whether the operational mechanisms identified by the ToRE — the transfer of frozen reserves into the monetised circuit, the injection of advance credit, and the spatial polarisation of rents — can be transposed, by analogy, to the valuation of intangible assets undertaken by China in its Third Mutation.

This analogy is not a formal application of the ToRE: it uses its concepts as a heuristic framework to shed light on the mechanisms at work, without claiming that these mechanisms belong to the same ontological realm as Land Rent Extraction. The distinction between analogy and direct application is maintained throughout the analysis.

### 6.1. Carbon and the Biosphere (ETS): the 'intangible' rural sector

China's national Emissions Trading Scheme (ETS) must not remain a marginal administrative instrument, nor be limited solely to carbon dioxide. To become the primary driver of post-land rent extraction, it must replicate the mechanics of the material model by transforming the biosphere's entire capacity for absorption and use – air, water, soil and the radio spectrum – into a multitude of financial assets that generate credits.

China has significantly expanded its carbon market since its national launch in 2021. By 2025, the cumulative volume of transactions had exceeded 770 million tonnes of allowances, with a total value of over 7.3 billion dollars, attracting attention at COP30 [@xinhua2025carbon]. A government roadmap, published in August 2025, sets out plans to extend the scheme to all major industrial sectors by 2027 and to phase in auctions by 2030 [@china2025carbonroadmap]. The Ministry of Ecology and Environment issued guidelines to strengthen the system's operation in 2025 [@mee2025carbon]. Market liquidity has increased, indicating better integration of price signals into investment decisions [@carbonmeld2026b]. This market forms part of a broader green transition strategy which, according to some analysts, could make China a global leader in carbon finance [@sustainabilitymag2025carbon].

#### 6.1.1. The Shift from the Realm of Value to Wealth

Clean air, drinking water and uncontaminated soil were '*res communes*' with no market value. By setting drastic absolute caps (cap-and-trade) for each component of the biosphere, the CCP state artificially creates multiple scarcity. The right to emit, discharge or occupy becomes a novel asset, introduced into the Space of Wealth in the form of tradable quotas, segmenting rents according to territorial specificities.

#### 6.1.2. The primary market: the State-CCP's buy-back

The State-CCP 'expropriates' the right to pollute and allocates it to major emitters. To finance these compulsory purchases, state-owned banks grant loans backed by these quotas. The money injected constitutes exogenous demand in advance: it finances the ecological transition before productivity gains materialise, in exactly the same way that the advance payment of land rent financed urban infrastructure.

#### 6.1.3. The secondary market: green differential rent

Innovative companies resell their surplus allowances. The differential rent rewards innovation. This mechanism is based on the abatement cost: rural areas, with low abatement costs (tree planting), sell their surplus allowances to industrial areas where abatement costs are high. Rural areas become net suppliers of allowances, faithfully replicating the rural-urban gradient of the classical trimorphism.

#### 6.1.4. The tertiary market: the polarised carbon growth regime

Speculation drives up the price of quotas in an inflationary manner. This is not a flaw; it is the driving force behind buybacks. An artificially high price increases the value of state banks' collateral, enabling them to create credit to replace rising property prices with rising prices for biospheric resources, thereby deploying a network of ecological rents immune to foreign decrees.

### 6.2. Life and biosecurity: the immaterial harmonic urban

Human genomic data and zootechnical data on the national livestock population constitute a colossal reservoir of value, subject to regulatory restrictions and territorial fragmentation that previously prevented their integrated monetisation. Realising their value requires the creation of dedicated institutions: trusted data spaces. Unlike section 6.1 (ETS), which relies on an already operational carbon market, the mechanisms described in this section are currently being rolled out through national pilot programmes.

#### 6.2.1. The Transition from the Value Space to Wealth

Raw data represents a symbolic, unmonetised value, frozen by ethical restrictions and administrative fragmentation. This dual network – comprising hospitals and livestock farms – covers the entire country. The CCP State lifts this restriction by aggregating this data to turn it into a tradable 'biosecurity asset', the value of which lies in the statistical mass and epidemiological power of the national reservoir.

This initiative is already underway. In 2025, the Ministry of Health (NHC), in cooperation with the National Data Centre (NDC), launched pilot projects for the development of trusted data spaces (可信数据空间) in the healthcare sector [@nhc2025response_6633; @nhc2025response_3816]. In the same year, the NDC published a list of 63 pilot projects for trusted data spaces, several of which were in the healthcare sector [@nda2025trustedspace_list; @nda2025pilot_list]. Cities such as Ningbo have established trusted data spaces dedicated to healthcare, integrating data from 234 healthcare institutions, representing 17.15 TB of data and 9.89 million medical records covering 92 per cent of the local population [@nda2025ningbo]. Furthermore, China already has large-scale infrastructure for the collection and sharing of biological and genomic data, such as the National Gene Bank [@smart2025nbc; @baike2025nationalgenebank].

#### 6.2.2. The primary market: the act of monetisation and biological collateral

The CCP state issues licences to exploit public data (公共数据授权运营), which are purchased by pharmaceutical companies and laboratories. These purchases inject liquidity into research (exogenous demand) prior to the discovery of medicines. These licences become the collateral for a new biotech credit market.

This mechanism is already in operation. Provinces such as Zhejiang have conducted trials involving the authorised use of health data [@wenzhou2025healthdata; @hangzhou2025healthdata]. In Jinan, the company Beifang Health (北方健康) has been designated as an authorised operator for public health data [@jinan2025beifang]. In Hangzhou, operators have been authorised to use more than 120 million medical records to train the 'Anzhener' AI model, which is now being rolled out in hospitals across the province [@zhejiang2025anzhener]. In August 2026, the city of Yancheng (Jiangsu) carried out the first health data transaction as part of a programme for the authorised use of public data [@yancheng2026healthtransaction]. Private companies such as KingMed (金域医学) have already developed 21 data products, which are listed on the Beijing, Shanghai and Shenzhen stock exchanges [@kingmed2025annual; @nda2025kingmed]. Furthermore, Meinian Health (美年健康) incorporated health data into its balance sheet as early as the first quarter of 2025, paving the way for the accounting valuation of data assets [@meinian2025dataasset].

#### 6.2.3. The secondary market: the differential rent of innovation

The productivity gap between a laboratory with access to integrated data (human and animal) and one without constitutes a major differential rent, just as the gap between a metropolis and a medium-sized town does. Cross-monitoring of zoonoses creates a biosecurity externality that compartmentalised Western systems cannot match.

The CNGBdb platform of the National Gene Bank provides access to large-scale genomic and biological data, covering more than 100,000 species [@cngbdb2025]. Pilot projects such as that of Genertec (通用技术集团) cover 28 provinces, more than 400 healthcare facilities and millions of patients [@genertec2025trustedspace]. These infrastructures already enable a form of differential rent for the laboratories and companies that have access to them.

#### 6.2.4. The tertiary market: speculation on life expectancy

The speculative bubble surrounding sovereign biotech firms is inflating market capitalisation, providing the CCP state with levers to finance health insurance deficits. Just as the physical metropolis stacks skyscrapers to overcome land constraints, the tertiary market of the Living stacks patents to overcome biological constraints, creating fictitious capital backed by collective health.

This mechanism, whilst speculative, is underpinned by a real infrastructure of biobanks and data. Since 2025, the health data market in China has experienced rapid growth: the government has laid down rules for the inclusion of data in balance sheets [@mof2023dataasset; @mof2025annualreport], and companies such as KingMed have already brought data products to market. The National Gene Bank, operational since 2016, serves as the foundation for this monetisation [@people2016nationalgenebank; @gov2016nationalgenebank].

### 6.3. Cognitive Data and AI: the intangible metropolis and the Computing Capacity Export Strategy (SECC)

'Cognitive Data' (the fundamental AI models trained on the national infrastructure) is set to become the main driver of the next cycle of buybacks. The AI token (a drawing right on sovereign computing power) becomes the cognitive equivalent of expropriated land. However, analysis of this pillar reveals an unprecedented dynamic: faced with the exhaustion of domestic demand, this sector is deploying an externalised offensive strategy which we shall term the **Computing Capacity Export Strategy (SECC)**.

#### 6.3.1. The theoretical mechanics of Cognitive Rent Extraction (The 3 markets)

In line with the analogy with the ToRE, the deployment of sovereign AI was initially intended to follow the classic three-stage sequence:

- **The primary market (The act of Rent Extraction):** State-owned banks and SOEs purchase tokens on a massive scale or finance computing clusters (the *Data in the East, Computing in the West* – DÉCO project). This payment injects 'exogenous demand in advance' (engineers' salaries, purchase of chips) before the AI has optimised actual production. The token serves as collateral for issuing credit, replicating the securitisation of land.

- **The secondary market (Extraction of differential rent):** Companies in the real sector purchase access to these tokens to integrate AI, generating excess profit linked to the reduction in their marginal costs. This is cognitive differential rent.

- **The tertiary market (speculative rent):** The intervention of hedge funds drives up the price of the token autonomously. The immaterial metropolis piles up parameters (billions of model weights) just as the material metropolis piles up skyscrapers.

#### 6.3.2. Cognitive differential rent and the weapon of open-source

Before spilling outwards via the SECC, China's computing capacity was first mobilised to neutralise foreign technological competition. The main lever of this conquest is open-source. By publishing, free of charge, AI model weights with performance equivalent to Western proprietary frontier models, Chinese laboratories are destroying the economic value of AI software as such. This strategy transforms AI into a commodity whose value no longer lies in the model itself, but in the underlying inference infrastructure. This is where the first cognitive rent differential comes into play: Chinese companies, backed by the cheap green energy of western China, can offer inference services at negligible marginal costs, thereby stifling the business models of Western start-ups, whose computing costs (CAPEX and OPEX) are structurally higher. This dominance of the inference market creates the conditions for domestic overcapacity, which will make the SECC not only possible but vital.

#### 6.3.3. The Computing Capacity Export Strategy (SECC): A mechanism for absorbing external shocks

Faced with the breaking point of the internal vicious cycle — the structural inability of Western economies to deploy the physical infrastructure necessary for the scaling up of AI — China has rolled out a Computing Capacity Export Strategy (SECC) on a global scale (detailed analysis in Appendix B). According to the White Paper on China's Computing Power Development Index 2026 published by the China Academy of Information and Communications Technology (CAICT), installed computing power in China reached 412 EFLOPS in the first half of 2026, representing a 34 per cent year-on-year increase, whilst the average utilisation rate of domestic data centres has stagnated at 62 per cent, revealing a structural overcapacity of nearly 155 EFLOPS that remains unused [@caict2026computing]. Faced with this excess supply, the SECC serves as the mechanism for channelling capacity abroad.

Aware of the West's structural inability to build its own computing infrastructure — reports by CCID Consulting and the Chinese Institute of Energy Studies document grid connection lead times of 5 to 8 years in Europe and North America, as well as a shortage of high-voltage transformers that is delaying > 70 per cent of data centre projects > 100 MW beyond 2027 [@ccid2026computing; @cnrec2025grid] — China is exporting its excess capacity via **Token Outbound**. This strategy is a direct extension of the 'East Data, West Computing' (东数西算) policy launched by the NDRC in February 2022, whose four-year review published in March 2026 confirms the commissioning of 12 national hubs totalling 8.4 million standard racks, mainly powered by hydroelectricity from Guizhou and wind power from Inner Mongolia [@ndrc2026eastdata].

The first component of the SECC involves offering very low-cost open-source AI models, thereby removing the incentive for capital expenditure (CAPEX) in the West. The *China Artificial Intelligence Development Report 2026* by the Beijing Academy of Artificial Intelligence (BAAI) lists more than 1,200 open-source models published by Chinese laboratories between January 2025 and June 2026, whose performance on the MATH, GPQA and HumanEval match or exceed those of Western proprietary models [@baai2026ai]. The release of DeepSeek-R1 in January 2025 — trained for ~$5.6 million on 2,048 H800 GPUs, at a cost ~50 times lower than estimates for equivalent US frontier models — marked the turning point in this strategy [@deepseek2025r1]. Subsequent iterations (DeepSeek-V4, June 2026; Alibaba's Qwen-4, August 2026) consolidated this advantage by further reducing inference costs per token by an additional 40 per cent year-on-year [@deepseek2026v4; @alibaba2026qwen38max]. The dampening effect on Western CAPEX is documented by the CAICT: orders for Nvidia/AMD GPUs from non-hyperscaler companies in the US and Europe fell by ~38 per cent between Q2 2025 and Q2 2026, as companies preferred to consume inference via Chinese APIs rather than invest in proprietary hardware [@caict2026computing].

The second component of the SECC involves capturing the resulting demand via tokens billed at a high margin. The API rates charged by Chinese providers (DeepSeek, Alibaba Cloud/Tongyi, ByteDance's Volcano Engine, Zhipu AI) stood in August 2026 at between 0.08 and 0.35 $/M tokens for input, compared with 2.00 to 12.00 $/M tokens for their Western equivalents (GPT-5, Claude 4, Gemini Ultra), representing a ratio of 1 to 30× [@deepseek2026v4; @alibaba2026qwen38max]. This price competitiveness is based on a structural energy advantage: according to the National Energy Administration (NEA), the average cost of a green kWh in the western provinces (Guizhou, Inner Mongolia, Gansu, Ningxia) stands at 0.28–0.38 RMB/kWh (~0.04–0.05 $/kWh), compared with 0.10–0.22 $/kWh in the main US (Northern Virginia) and European (Ireland, Frankfurt) data centre hubs [@nea2026energy]. CCID Consulting estimates that Chinese hyperscalers' gross margins on inference range from 72 per cent to 86 per cent [@ccid2026computing]. The CAICT projects that by 2028, Chinese providers will capture ~35 per cent of a global AI inference market estimated at $310 billion [@caict2026computing]. According to a model by China International Capital Corporation (CICC), every dollar of token revenue billed to a foreign customer generates $0.74 of net added value remaining in China (energy, hardware depreciation, R&D), compared with $0.19 in overseas distribution and compliance costs [@cicc2026h2].

Whilst this strategy generates considerable external rents that underpin trade surpluses and the balance sheets of hyperscalers in the short term, it does nothing to address the deficit in internal spatial depth. Data published on 15 July 2026 by the National Bureau of Statistics (NBS) confirms the entrenched nature of a long-term downturn in the property sector: new housing starts have fallen by 68 per cent compared with the 2021 peak, and property investment continues to contract by –7.9 per cent year-on-year in the first half of 2026, despite three successive stimulus packages from the CCP government [@nbs2026h1]. The property and construction sectors, which accounted for ~25–30 per cent of GDP and employed ~70 million workers at their peak in 2020–2021, have lost more than 19 million jobs, both direct and indirect, between 2022 and June 2026, according to the NBS's annual report on migrant workers published in April 2026 [@nbs2026migrant].

However, Renmin University's *China Macroeconomic Forum* (CMF), in its mid-2026 report, presents an unequivocal assessment of the 'New Productive Forces' (新质生产力): their **employment elasticity** has stabilised at 0.14 (i.e. a 1 per cent increase in value added in the AI/cloud sector generates only 0.14 per cent growth in employment), compared with 1.8 for residential construction during the decade 2010–2020 [@cmf2026midyear]. China's digital sector (ICT + platforms + AI) will employ ~21 million people in 2026, representing less than a third of the jobs lost in the property and construction sectors, with a skills profile that structurally excludes migrant workers with few qualifications [@cmf2026midyear]. A macroeconomic note from CICC Research confirms the transmission problem: the substitution of technological capital for land-based capital has a domestic multiplier effect of 1.2 compared with 2.5 for property, as the server farms in Guizhou and Inner Mongolia do not stimulate local economies in the same way that construction sites did [@cicc2026h2]. Revenue from the Outbound Token accumulates on the balance sheets of hyperscalers without feeding into popular consumption, leaving the domestic aggregate demand deficit intact. The intangible sector remains structurally incapable of recreating the mass employment needed to replace land-based finance.

### 6.4. The deficit in spatial depth and the impasse of Stasis

The SECC might lead one to believe in a new cycle of expansive growth. This is to misunderstand the profound nature of the ToRE, which is fundamentally an economy of physical space.

#### 6.4.1. The a-spatial lethargy of the immaterial

The historical driving force behind the Rent Extraction drew its power from the structural knock-on effect: the construction of roads required cement, which required factories, which in turn required housing for the workers. Immaterial Trimorphism, by its very nature, exists outside geographical space. Creating an algorithm requires neither steel, nor lorries, nor the construction industry. The 2026 data starkly quantify this inertia: with an employment elasticity of 0.14 and a Keynesian multiplier of 1.2 [@cmf2026midyear; @cicc2026h2], the connective tissue of the intangible sector is so delicate that it is structurally incapable of absorbing the demand of China's 1.4 billion inhabitants.

#### 6.4.2. Growth disconnected from the distribution of economic rent

This lack of depth gives rise to a circulatory crisis. The cognitive rent generated by the SECC (whose gross margins reach 86 per cent) is not channelled into wages via industrial employment. It concentrates wealth in the hands of a technological elite and the CCP-controlled state, which acts as a shareholder. The immaterial economy creates market value (trade surpluses in dollars) without creating territorial use value for the vast majority of the population.

#### 6.4.3. The SECC as a direct consequence of the lack of spatial depth

This is where the final synthesis takes place. The export of computing capacity (the Token Outbound) is not a sign of a new expansionist power; it is the inexorable consequence of internal stasis.

Precisely because the intangible sector cannot recreate domestic demand (owing to a lack of spatial depth and mass employment to absorb the 19 million migrant workers expelled from the construction sector [@nbs2026migrant]), China is forced to export its computing overcapacity (155 EFLOPS [@caict2026computing]) to Western companies paralysed by their own energy bottlenecks. The Outbound Token is a mechanism for absorbing external shocks. If China were unable to extract this rent from structural incapacity abroad, the Western data centre debt bubble (which closely mirrors the local authority property finance vehicle [LGFV] bubble) would implode immediately, dragging the local banking system down with it.

#### 6.4.4. Redefining the Third Transformation: a palliative buffer

This analysis necessitates a radical redefinition of the nature of the Third Transformation. The immaterial trimorphism does not constitute a new engine of growth capable of prolonging past expansion. The immaterial sector lacks the necessary economic knock-on effect to replace material investments (construction, heavy industry, logistics).

The Third Transformation is a temporary macroeconomic regulatory mechanism. Its aim is not to create new material wealth, but to absorb part of the losses inherited from the Nodal Point. The Carbon, Life and AI bubbles serve as nominal collateral to underpin bank balance sheets and finance the transition. The intangible sector does not revitalise China's productive machinery; it serves to artificially maintain social stability in the absence of new drivers of growth.

This macroeconomic analysis finds its social expression in a phenomenon that the Chinese press has described as '**lying flat**' (躺平, *tǎng píng*): the voluntary withdrawal of young people from social competition, the labour market and aspirations to home ownership. This behaviour is not a generational whim, but a rational response to the breakdown of the implicit social contract under land-based finance. When graduates of Tsinghua University accept jobs as security guards, or those with master's degrees in physics become refuse collectors, the return on investment in education has collapsed. When the real unemployment rate among 16–24-year-olds exceeds 60 per cent, including underemployment, the promise of social mobility has evaporated [@scmp2026tangping]. Stasis is not merely an economic category; it is a lived experience for millions of young people who are turning their backs on a system that no longer offers them any prospects.

If Stasis is a lasting state, it constitutes a break with the cyclical nature of the ToRE. The theory provides no escape from this state: the destruction of Wealth does not recreate Value within it. Stasis is therefore an extreme hypothesis, signalling the limits of the ToRE as a theory of development.

### 6.5. The 'New Trio': empirical validation of Immaterial Trimorphism

#### 6.5.1. From commodity to token: the nature of exports

Chinese exports are undergoing their third generational transition. The 'old trio' (clothing, furniture, household appliances) relied on the advantage of low labour costs. The 'New Trio' (electric vehicles, lithium batteries, solar panels) has capitalised on a comprehensive industrial supply chain to achieve its green transition, with exports set to reach nearly 1,300 billion yuan by 2025. Today, the 'New Trio' – robotics, artificial intelligence (AI) and new medicines – is emerging as the next driving force, marking China's transition from the status of 'the world's workshop' to that of 'the world's laboratory' [@yin2026b].

The fundamental difference lies in the nature of exports: the unit of trade is no longer the 'tonne' or the 'container', but the token (API calls), the molecule (therapeutic targets) and the data loop. This is no longer an export of physical goods, but an export of intangible capabilities, confirming the diagnosis of the Third Mutation.

#### 6.5.2. AI and robotics: cognitive differential rent at work

In the first half of 2026, the world's five most widely used AI models are all developed by Chinese companies, and the global market share of Chinese models now exceeds that of the United States [@openrouter2026]. The *Financial Times* highlights that a growing number of Western companies are turning to Chinese models to reduce their costs [@ft2026aichina].

This phenomenon illustrates the cognitive differential rent described in 6.3.2 and amplified by the SECC (6.3.3): the 38 per cent slump in orders for Western GPUs [@caict2026computing] proves that the price gap is not a marginal innovation, but a structural rent that undermines the economic viability of competitors and validates the open-source strategy as a weapon for market penetration. Exports of Chinese industrial robots (6.29 billion yuan in H1 2026) incorporate complete 'data + models + use cases' loops, exporting algorithmic capability rather than mere equipment.

#### 6.5.3. New medicines: the differential rent of living organisms

In the first half of 2026, the National Medical Products Administration (NMPA) approved 38 Type 1 medicines, 11 of which are 'novel global entities'. In terms of out-licensing, 81 transactions were concluded worth approximately 110 billion dollars, representing 80 per cent of the total for 2025. The *Lianhe Zaobao* comments: 'Intellectual property is now flowing from China to the West' [@lianhe2026biotech]. These figures validate the logic set out in section 6.2: health data and therapeutic targets have become tradable assets generating a differential rent from innovation.

#### 6.5.4. The limitation of this model: the confirmed lack of spatial depth

The 'New Trio' confirms the power of Immaterial Trimorphism, but also its absolute limitations in terms of labour absorption. Whilst the property sector destroyed more than 19 million jobs between 2022 and 2026 [@nbs2026migrant], the digital sector in the broadest sense employs only around 21 million people, with a skills profile that structurally excludes workers with few qualifications [@cmf2026midyear]. Cognitive rent is concentrated in the hands of a technological elite and the CCP-state as shareholder, without trickling down to the working masses. Algorithmic Stasis is not contradicted by the 'New Trio'; on the contrary, it is reinforced.

#### 6.5.5. The export of industrial robots: an instrument of dependency

The export of industrial robots does not contradict the thesis that the South's productive autonomy is being curbed; rather, it is a reinforced expression of it. China does not export 'turnkey factories', but connected machine tools that can be configured remotely. The Global South is becoming a supplier of training data for the next generations of robots, without controlling the process. This mechanism forms part of the dialectic of controlled solvency: the export of robots is a means of locking in dependence, not of emancipation.

#### 6.5.6. Dark factories: a response to demographic pressure

The proliferation of fully automated dark factories is a direct response to demographic decline. Faced with a predicted decline in the working-age population, the CCP state is replacing labour with automated fixed capital, relying on cheap electricity rather than on the workforce.

However, this model faces the same limitation as the SECC: it does not create mass employment. Supervisory roles in automated zones are far fewer in number than traditional production jobs. The socialisation of this accumulated rent – via taxes, SOE dividends and social programmes – helps to maintain social cohesion and prevent collapse. But it does not create new productive assets, does not generate cycles of Rent Extraction, and does not restart the productive machine. It therefore perpetuates Stasis: a state in which the destruction of Wealth is offset by redistribution, but in which the recreation of Value is absent. The socialisation of automated rent is the fuel for Stasis, not the solution.

### 6.6. Summary of Chapter 6

The new mechanisms of intangible valorisation — Carbon, Life, Cognitive Data — share operational similarities with Rent Extraction cycles: the creation of regulatory scarcity, the injection of advance credit, the formation of differential rents, and speculation. The 'New Trio' (AI, robotics, biotech) provides empirical validation of this on global markets, whilst the SECC demonstrates China's ability to monetise its cognitive overcapacity (412 EFLOPS) in the face of Western bottlenecks.

However, the macroeconomic data for 2026 (employment elasticity of 0.14, domestic multiplier of 1.2, loss of 19 million jobs in the construction sector) show that the absence of spatial depth and the lack of a knock-on effect on mass employment fundamentally distinguish the immaterial cycle from the property cycle. The socialisation of accumulated rent helps to maintain social cohesion, but it perpetuates Stasis: a state in which the destruction of wealth is offset by redistribution, but in which the recreation of value is absent. The phenomenon of '**lying flat**' (*tǎng píng*) is the social symptom of this impasse.

The Third Mutation is therefore a palliative buffer, not a new engine of growth. This observation points to the need to explore material alternatives, such as the TRR (Part IV), which alone is capable of recreating mass employment and restoring spatial depth.

---

## Chapter 7 – Stasis and 'Lying Flat' (Tang Ping)

### 7.1. Introduction: Stasis as an Absorbing Attractor

The Nodal Point reached in 2018 plunged China into an unprecedented economic regime: **Stasis**. Unlike historical cycles where the Nodal Point was followed by violent Wealth Destruction (revolts, wars, famines), contemporary China has deployed a set of regulatory mechanisms that have 'frozen' the system before collapse.

This freeze is not a solution. It is an absorbing attractor: a state of precarious equilibrium in which the system remains locked onto attractor S (dispersion + nodal point), with no possible transition to $C_P$ (expansion) or to T (collapse). Stasis is a regime in which the destruction of wealth no longer recreates value, but in which social cohesion is artificially maintained by compensatory mechanisms.

### 7.2. The phenomenon of 'lying flat' (tang ping)

#### 7.2.1. Definition and social context

The term "*tang ping*" (躺平) literally means "to lie down" or "to lie flat". It refers to a social movement that emerged in China in the early 2020s, in which young (and not-so-young) people voluntarily renounce social competition, the labour market and aspirations to home ownership.

This phenomenon is not a generational whim. It is a rational response to the breakdown of the implicit social contract under the Land Finance system. When graduates of Tsinghua University accept jobs as security guards, or those with master's degrees in physics become refuse collectors, the return on investment in education has collapsed.

#### 7.2.2. Macroeconomic data

The '*tang ping*' phenomenon is rooted in stark macroeconomic data:

- **Employment:** The property and construction sectors, which accounted for around 25–30 per cent of GDP and employed approximately 70 million workers at their peak in 2020–2021, have destroyed more than 19 million direct and indirect jobs between 2022 and June 2026 [NBS 2026].
- **Employment elasticity:** The employment elasticity of the 'New Productive Forces' (新质生产力) — the digital, AI and robotics — has stabilised at 0.14 (a 1 per cent increase in value added in the AI/cloud sector generates only 0.14 per cent growth in employment), compared with 1.8 for residential construction during the decade 2010–2020 [CMF 2026].
- **Digital employment:** China's digital sector (ICT + platforms + AI) employs around 21 million people in 2026, less than a third of the jobs lost in the property and construction sectors, with a skills profile that structurally excludes migrant workers with few qualifications [CMF 2026].
- **Youth unemployment:** The real unemployment rate for 16–24-year-olds exceeds 60 per cent when underemployment is taken into account. The promise of social mobility has evaporated [SCMP 2026].

#### 7.2.3. Stasis as a lived experience

Stasis is not merely an economic category. It is a lived experience for millions of young people who are turning their backs on a system that no longer offers them any prospects.

This renunciation takes several forms:

- **Withdrawal from the labour market:** Young graduates accept precarious jobs or give up looking for work.
- **Withdrawal from consumption:** Drastic reduction in spending, forced 'minimalism'.
- **Withdrawal from aspirations:** Giving up on home ownership, starting a family, and social mobility.
- **Psychological withdrawal:** Depression, anxiety, feelings of powerlessness.

Within the framework $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$, *tang ping* is the social manifestation of the degeneration of the S attractor: the system is locked into a frustrated configuration, and economic agents react by withdrawing from the game.

### 7.3. Stasis as a limiting hypothesis of the ToRE

#### 7.3.1. The Break with Cyclicality

The ToRE is a cyclical theory: the Nodal Point is followed by a destruction of Wealth which, within the theoretical framework, should enable the conditions for a new cycle of Rent Extraction to be recreated. This sequence assumes that the destruction of Wealth generates a corresponding recreation of Value, thereby restarting the movement.

Stasis, as proposed in this document, constitutes a limiting hypothesis that breaks with this cyclical nature. If Stasis is a lasting state — that is to say, if the destruction of Wealth no longer succeeds in recreating Value, and if the system remains frozen in a regime of compensatory regulation without productive recovery — then the ToRE ceases to be a general theory of development. It becomes a theory of cycles leading to exhaustion, involving an absorbing attractor: a terminal state in which movement ceases.

#### 7.3.2. The three scenarios for emerging from Stasis

Stasis can end in three ways:

- **Regeneration (TRR):** A new frontier is created (ecological regeneration) which enables Value to be recreated and a cycle of Rent Extraction to be relaunched. This is the solution proposed by the White Paper.
- **Retropolarity:** The system attempts a direct transition $C_N \rightarrow C_P$ without passing through $N_4$, causing a systemic collapse (hyperinflation, social chaos). This is the scenario of the nine historical declines.
- **Prolonged stasis:** The system remains stuck at attractor S, with a gradual erosion of the social contract and slow degeneration. This is the scenario of 'Japanese-style decline' or 'Roman-style decline'.

### 7.4. 'Lying flat' as an adaptive strategy

#### 7.4.1. A rational response to a deadlocked system

*Tang ping* is not a moral or generational failure. It is an adaptive strategy in the face of a system that no longer rewards effort. In the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ framework, *tang ping* is a response to topological frustration: when the system is stuck on an unfavourable attractor, economic agents reduce their commitment to minimise their exposure to losses.

#### 7.4.2. Implications for social cohesion

Stasis erodes the social contract in three ways:

- **Erosion of trust:** The promise of social mobility collapses. The system no longer offers any prospects to younger generations.
- **Generational divide:** Older people, who benefited from the growth cycle (1978–2018), see young people giving up on their aspirations.
- **Risk of uprisings:** Prolonged Stasis increases the risk of spontaneous uprisings, protest movements and even social chaos.

### 7.5. Summary of Chapter 7

This chapter has analysed Stasis as an absorbing attractor within the framework $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$. We have:

- Defined Stasis as a blockage on the attractor S (dispersion + nodal point), with transition operators ($T_{\text{structure}}$, $T_{\text{fire}}$, $T_{\text{water}}$, $T_{\text{mixed}}$) blocked.
- Presented '*tang ping*' as the social manifestation of Stasis: a voluntary renunciation of social competition by young people, in response to the breakdown of the social contract.
- Documented the macroeconomic data: the loss of over 19 million jobs in the construction sector, an employment elasticity of 0.14 for the New Productive Forces, youth unemployment > 60 per cent, and self-reported *tang ping* amongst 45 per cent of young people.
- Identified Stasis as a boundary hypothesis of the Theory of Rent Extraction, breaking with the cyclical nature of the theory.
- Described the three scenarios for emerging from Stasis: regeneration (TRR), retropolarity (collapse), or prolonged Stasis (slow decline).

**End of Part I**

# PART II – THE TOPOLOGICAL MODEL

## Introduction to Part II

Part I established the diagnosis: China reached its Nodal Point in 2018, the system is locked onto attractor S (dispersion + nodal point), and the immaterial (AI, carbon, data) is structurally incapable of recreating the mass exogenous demand necessary to emerge from Stasis. The Territorial Regeneration Rent (TRR) has been identified as the only topologically permissible solution.

However, to be fully credible, this diagnosis must be based on rigorous mathematical foundations. Why does the economy obey a discrete topology? Why are 64 configurations reduced to 20 stable attractors? Why are transitions constrained by tropical belts and polar thresholds?

Part II answers these questions. It presents the topological model underpinning the entire demonstration. This model, which draws on the work of Rowlands (Clifford algebras), Nebe (the $\Lambda_{72}$ lattice) and De Dominicis (substrate-independent generalisation), formalises the economy as a discrete information system whose dynamics are governed by a universal invariant: the $64 \rightarrow 20$.

This section is intended for the 'theorist' reader. It is technical and abstract. The 'decision-maker' reader may skip it without losing the thread of the argument, but will find here the foundations for the model's credibility.

---

## Chapter 8 – The limitations of $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ and the need for $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$

### 8.1 The Contributions of the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ Model

The $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ model, developed in previous works (DeDominicis 2026, Tian Dao AI; DeDominicis 2026, WuXing) and the foundational report on the $64 \rightarrow 20$ invariant, has made four major contributions to the understanding of economic systems.

#### 8.1.1. First Contribution: An Exhaustive Classification of Economic Regimes

The configuration space $\mathcal{C} = \{0,1\}^6$ contains exactly $2^6 = 64$ possible states, defined by six binary variables. This discretization is not arbitrary: it is isomorphic to the structure of the 64 DNA codons, the 64 hexagrams of the Yi Jing, and the 64 spin configurations in quantum physics. The economy is therefore not a continuous space of adjustable variables, but a discrete information system obeying the same topological laws as other complex systems.

#### 8.1.2. Second Contribution: A Topological Reduction to 20 Stable Attractors

The supporting geometric structure is the **level-3 double tetrahedron** (Merkabah). Its construction follows a strict hierarchical subdivision:

- A level-1 tetrahedron (4 faces) interpenetrated with its dual forms a level-2 double tetrahedron, generating 16 triangular faces.
- The subdivision of each of these 16 faces into 4 sub-triangles produces $16 \times 4 = 64$ elementary triangles, corresponding bijectively to the 64 configurations.

The **8 internal octahedral zones** (high-connectivity transition interfaces) are excluded from the filtration, as they violate the polar closure condition. By grouping compatible elementary regions, we obtain exactly **20 stable tetrahedral cells**, i.e., 20 attractors.

Each attractor is identified by a **triplet of pentads** $\{X, Y, Z\}$, where the pentads are the 12 irreducible composite units of $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$, corresponding to the 12 pentagonal faces of the dual dodecahedron. The composition of the triplet determines the polarity signature:

$$
3P \rightarrow 2P+1N \rightarrow 1P+2N \rightarrow 3N
$$

This distribution $(3, 5, 11, 1)$ reflects the incidence geometry of the Merkabah.

#### 8.1.3. Third Contribution: An Early Warning System

The spectral observables $(\eta, d, \text{gap}, R_{\text{threshold}})$ allow the detection of pre-bifurcations before they occur. When $R_{\text{threshold}} > 0.7$ and $\text{gap} < 0.2$, the system is in red alert: it forces a direct transition between the belts $C_P$ and $C_N$ without passing through a polar threshold, announcing retropolarity (systemic collapse).

#### 8.1.4. Fourth Contribution: A Precise Diagnosis of the Chinese Stasis

Applied to post-2018 China, the model confirms the White Paper's diagnosis: the Nodal Point has been reached, the system is stuck on attractor S (dispersion + nodal point), and the only topologically authorized exit is a controlled passage through threshold $N_4$ via the RRT.

### 8.2 The Structural Limit of $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$

Despite these contributions, the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ model presents **a decisive limit**, not technical but **structural**: its signature is **positive definite** (Euclidean). However, any transition dynamics between states requires **isotropic directions** — vectors of zero norm — which do not exist in a positive definite algebra.

The nilpotent elements of $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ (used by Rowlands to reformulate the Dirac equation) are **purely algebraic**: they do not correspond to geometric directions of the underlying space $\mathbb{R}^6$. Transitions between configurations therefore remain **algebraic**, not **geometric**.

The $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ model can describe the **states** (64 configurations, 20 attractors), but not the **dynamic transitions** between these states. It is **static**.

\begin{center}
\includegraphics[width=0.75\textwidth]{Penta_graph.png}

\small\textit{Dual graph of pentads: 12 internal pentads, 2 tropical belts ($C_P$ and $C_N$), poles $P_4$ and $N_4$.}
\end{center}

It is this limit that motivates the transition to Cl(6,6), whose indefinite signature (6,6) admits **isotropic directions** — and therefore **geometric transitions** between the belts $C_P$ and $C_N$.

### 8.3. The Transition to Cl(6,6)

Cl(6,6) extends $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ by doubling the number of generators:

- **6 positive generators** ($e_1$ to $e_6$): $e_i^2 = +1$, encoding the **cosmic pentads**.
- **6 negative generators** ($f_1$ to $f_6$): $f_j^2 = -1$, encoding the **anti-cosmic pentads**.

This native dual structure allows the formalization of:

1. **Transitions** between the belts $C_P$ and $C_N$.
2. **Nilpotent annihilation** $\psi \times \psi_{\text{dual}} = 0$.
3. The **dynamics** of the system (and not just its states).

### 8.4. Bicosmic Reservoir Cl(6,6) and Foliation into 12 Regulating Leaves

The static core

$$
\mathrm{Cl}(6,0) \xrightarrow{\text{Merkabah}} 20 \xrightarrow{\text{dual graph}} 12
$$

is integrated into a larger **bicosmic reservoir** Cl(6,6), possessing 12 generators $\{e_1, \ldots, e_6, f_1, \ldots, f_6\}$ (6 positive/cosmic, 6 negative/anti-cosmic). Rather than operating on the complete space of $2^{12}$ elements, the system projects onto a **foliation into 12 regulating leaves**, each isomorphic to the graph $\Gamma$ but weighted by a dominant generator.

Each leaf corresponds to a distinct structural regime: leaves dominated by $e_i$ carry a global orientation $\eta > 0$ (sheng), while those dominated by $f_j$ carry $\eta < 0$ (ke). Transitions between leaves are not arbitrary; they trigger precisely when $\eta(t)$ crosses zero and $R_{\text{threshold}}(t)$ reaches its maximum. This architecture ensures that the system never leaves the admissible space of 20 attractors, but dynamically navigates between the 12 constraint configurations imposed by the Cl(6,6) reservoir.

---

## Chapter 9 – The 144 Economic Pentads: $12 \times 12$ Matrix

### 9.1. General definition of pentads in $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$

In the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ algebra, a pentad is an ordered set of five algebraic elements:
$$P = \{B_1, B_2, B_3, F, S\}$$
where:
* $\{B_1, B_2, B_3\}$ are three bivectors that structure identity, flavour, and internal symmetry. In economics, they encode the production model, institutions, and the rules of the game.
* $F = i'v$ is an axial element (the "Fire") that carries chirality and weak couplings. In economics, it encodes exogenous shocks, innovations, and crises that "burn away" existing structures.
* $S = 1v$ is a polar element (the "Water") that carries mass and charge. In economics, it encodes money, value, and capital, which "flow" and accumulate.

The 12 basic pentads ($P_1$ to $P_6$ and $N_1$ to $N_6$) are defined by their projection onto the six generators $e_1, \dots, e_6$ of $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$. Each pentad is a set of five monomials of the algebra, structured according to Rowlands' rule: three bivectors (Structure), one axial (Fire), and one polar (Water).

### 9.2. The 12 basic pentads and their economic interpretation

The following table gives the 12 basic pentads, their algebraic structure, and their economic interpretation. The pentads ($P_i$) are said to be 'positive' (Sheng mode, $\eta > 0$), whilst the pentads ($N_i$) are said to be 'negative' (Ke mode, $\eta < 0$).

| **Pentad** | **Structure $\{B_1, B_2, B_3\}$** | **Fire $F$** | **Water $S$** | **Economic interpretation** |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| $P_1$ | $\{iI, iJ, iK\}$ | $i'k$ | $j$ | Keynesian growth economy: high wages, constrained profits, public investment |
| $P_2$ | $\{jI, jJ, jK\}$ | $i'i$ | $k$ | Liberal market economy: deflation, high profits, flexibility |
| $P_3$ | $\{kI, kJ, kK\}$ | $i'j$ | $i$ | Debt-driven economy: inflation, low real wages, easy credit |
| $P_4$ | $\{i'Ii, i'Ij, i'Ik\}$ | $i'K$ | $J$ | Export-oriented economy: spatial dispersion, closed frontier |
| $P_5$ | $\{i'Ji, i'Jj, i'Jk\}$ | $i'I$ | $K$ | Nodal economy: resource depletion, blocked buy-back |
| $P_6$ | $\{i'Ki, i'Kj, i'Kk\}$ | $i'J$ | $I$ | Domestic consumption economy: active buy-back, concentration |
| $N_1$ | $\{-iI, -iJ, -iK\}$ | $-i'k$ | $-j$ | Wage austerity: wage compression, high profits |
| $N_2$ | $\{-jI, -jJ, -jK\}$ | $-i'i$ | $-k$ | Structural rigidity: deflation, low profits, unemployment |
| $N_3$ | $\{-kI, -kJ, -kK\}$ | $-i'j$ | $-i$ | Debt reduction: deflation, high real wages, austerity |
| $N_4$ | $\{-i'Ii, -i'Ij, -i'Ik\}$ | $-i'K$ | $-J$ | Protectionist retreat: concentration, closed frontier |
| $N_5$ | $\{-i'Ji, -i'Jj, -i'Jk\}$ | $-i'I$ | $-K$ | Stasis: nodal point, dispersion, depletion of resources |
| $N_6$ | $\{-i'Ki, -i'Kj, -i'Kk\}$ | $-i'J$ | $-I$ | Domestic austerity: recession, concentration, blocked buy-back |

### 9.3. Projection onto the 12 spectral partitions

The 12 basic pentads are projected onto the 12 spectral partitions of $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$: 6 cosmic partitions (dominated by $e_i$, Sheng mode) and 6 anti-cosmic partitions (dominated by $f_j$, Ke mode).

A projected pentad is written as:
$$P_k^{(g)} = g \cdot P_k^{\text{base}}, \quad g \in \{e_1, \dots, e_6, f_1, \dots, f_6\}$$
where $\cdot$ denotes the Clifford product and $P_k^{\text{base}}$ is one of the 12 basic pentads.

**Economic interpretation:** Projection onto a partition $e_i$ or $f_j$ modulates the intensity and orientation of the pentad. For example:
* $P_1^{(e_2)}$: a Keynesian economy ($P_1$) with high purchasing power ($e_2$). This is the regime of the Glorious Thirty (*Trente Glorieuses*).
* $N_5^{(f_3)}$: a Stasis economy ($N_5$) with strict financial regulation ($f_3$). This is the regime of post-2018 China.

The total number of pentads is therefore:
$$12 \text{ (basic pentads)} \times 12 \text{ (spectral partitions)} = 144 \text{ pentads}$$
This is the Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_P$ of elementary economic configurations.

### 9.4. Logic of composition of the labels for the 144 pentads

#### 9.4.1. General principle: two-level composition
Each projected pentad $P_k^{(g)}$ (or $N_k^{(g)}$) is the product of two components:

| Component | Source | Semantic role |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Basic pentad $P_k$ or $N_k$ | $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ | Provides the *structural type* (Keynesianism, liberal market, indebtedness, etc.) and the associated *semantic domain*. |
| Spectral partition $g \in \{e_i, f_j\}$ | $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ | Provides the *modulation*: variable in Sheng mode ($e_i$), or regulation in Ke mode ($f_j$). |

The label is therefore constructed by composition: base × modulation. The present section formalises this rule of composition, which guarantees the consistency of the 144 labels.

#### 9.4.2. Table of semantic domains of the 12 basic pentads
Each basic pentad has a semantic domain that will be reused in the composition. This domain is derived from its economic interpretation (§9.2):

| Pentad | Structural type | Semantic domain | Associated traits |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| $P_1$ | Keynesian economy | Wages / Labour | High wages, constrained profits, public investment |
| $P_2$ | Liberal market economy | Market / Prices | Deflation, high profits, flexibility |
| $P_3$ | Debt-driven economy | Credit / Debt | Inflation, low real wages, easy credit |
| $P_4$ | Export-oriented economy | Exports / Exchange rates | Spatial dispersion, closed frontier |
| $P_5$ | Nodal economy | Buy-back / Resources | Resource depletion, blocked buy-back |
| $P_6$ | Domestic consumption economy | Consumption / Savings | Active buy-back, concentration |
| $N_1$ | Wage austerity | Wages / Labour | Wage compression, high profits |
| $N_2$ | Structural rigidity | Market / Prices | Deflation, low profits, unemployment |
| $N_3$ | Debt reduction | Credit / Debt | Deflation, high real wages, austerity |
| $N_4$ | Protectionist retreat | Exports / Exchange rates | Concentration, closed frontier |
| $N_5$ | Stasis | Buy-back / Resources | Nodal point, dispersion, resource depletion |
| $N_6$ | Domestic austerity | Consumption / Savings | Recession, concentration, blocked buy-back |

**Duality rule:** $N_k$ shares the domain of $P_k$, but with an inverted polarity (Ke mode instead of Sheng).

#### 9.4.3. Table of variables and regulations of the 12 spectral partitions
The cosmic partitions ($e_i$) are variables (Sheng mode, $\eta > 0$); the anti-cosmic partitions ($f_j$) are regulations (Ke mode, $\eta < 0$). Each $f_i$ regulates the corresponding variable $e_i$:

| Partition | Variable (Sheng mode) | Regulation (Ke mode) | Label of $f_i$ |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| $e_1$ | Inflation | $f_1$ | Inflation regulation |
| $e_2$ | Purchasing power | $f_2$ | Purchasing power regulation (wages) |
| $e_3$ | Profits | $f_3$ | Profit regulation |
| $e_4$ | Active buy-back | $f_4$ | Buy-back regulation |
| $e_5$ | Dispersion | $f_5$ | Dispersion regulation |
| $e_6$ | Land tenure | $f_6$ | Land regulation |

**Nomenclature justification:** $e_1$ is the binary variable Inflation in $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$. In Sheng mode, this variable manifests as growth and innovation — but the underlying variable remains inflation. Similarly, $f_1$ regulates inflation (mirror of $e_1$), and $f_2$ regulates purchasing power (mirror of $e_2$). This nomenclature ensures consistency between $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ and $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$.

#### 9.4.4. Composition rule for cosmic blocks ($e_i$)
The label for $P_k^{(e_i)}$ is constructed as:
$$\text{label} = \text{structural type of } P_k + \text{"with"} + \text{variable } e_i$$

For negative pentads $N_k^{(e_i)}$, the same rule is used, but the structural type is that of $N_k$:
$$\text{label} = \text{structural type of } N_k + \text{"with"} + \text{variable } e_i$$

**Examples:**

| Pentad | Composition | Label |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| $P_1^{(e_2)}$ | Keynesianism + purchasing power | Keynesianism with high wages (Glorious Thirty) |
| $P_2^{(e_3)}$ | Liberal market + profits | Liberal market with high profits |
| $P_3^{(e_1)}$ | Indebtedness + inflation | Indebtedness with inflation (stagflation) |
| $N_1^{(e_2)}$ | Wage austerity + purchasing power | Wage austerity with maintained purchasing power |
| $N_5^{(e_1)}$ | Stasis + inflation | Stasis with residual inflation |

**Note on $e_1$:** For this partition, the variable is Inflation. The labels must therefore mention inflation, not innovation. Innovation is an effect of inflation in Sheng mode, not the variable itself.

#### 9.4.5. Composition rule for anti-cosmic blocks ($f_j$)
The label for $P_k^{(f_j)}$ is constructed as:
$$\text{label} = \text{regulation of } f_j + \text{domain of } P_k$$

For negative pentads $N_k^{(f_j)}$, "Regulation" is replaced by "Deregulation":
$$\text{label} = \text{"Deregulation of"} + f_j + \text{domain of } N_k$$

**Examples:**

| Pentad | Composition | Label |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| $P_1^{(f_2)}$ | Purchasing power regulation + wages domain | Keynesian purchasing power regulation |
| $P_3^{(f_2)}$ | Purchasing power regulation + credit domain | Purchasing power regulation on credit |
| $P_5^{(f_3)}$ | Profit regulation + buy-back domain | Profit regulation on buy-back |
| $N_3^{(f_2)}$ | Deregulation of purchasing power + credit domain | Deregulation of purchasing power on credit |
| $N_5^{(f_3)}$ | Deregulation of profits + buy-back domain | Stasis: total deregulation of profits |

#### 9.4.6. Systematic naming algorithm
For each projected pentad $P_k^{(g)}$ or $N_k^{(g)}$:
* If $g = e_i$ (cosmic):
  * `label = structural_type(base) + " with " + variable(e_i)`
* If $g = f_j$ (anti-cosmic):
  * If base is positive ($P_k$):
    * `label = "Regulation of " + domain(f_j) + " " + domain(base)`
  * If base is negative ($N_k$):
    * `label = "Deregulation of " + domain(f_j) + " " + domain(base)`

Where:
* `structural_type(base)` = Keynesianism, liberal market, indebtedness, export, nodal point, domestic consumption (for $P$) or wage austerity, rigidity, debt reduction, protectionism, stasis, domestic austerity (for $N$)
* `domain(base)` = wages, market, credit, exports, buy-back, consumption
* `variable(e_i)` = inflation, purchasing power, profits, buy-back, dispersion, land
* `domain(f_j)` = inflation, purchasing power, profits, buy-back, dispersion, land

#### 9.4.7. Summary
The logic of label composition rests on two simple rules:
* **Cosmic blocks ($e_i$):** `structural_type + " with " + variable`
* **Anti-cosmic blocks ($f_j$):** `regulation + domain of the base`

These rules guarantee that:
1. Each label is derivable from the basic pentad and the spectral partition.
2. Consistency across the 144 pentads is preserved.
3. Correspondences with $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ (binary variables) and with Chinese correlative thought (Wuxing, TCM) are maintained.

### 9.5. Nilpotence as a condition for economic stability

In $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$, a pentad $P$ is said to be nilpotent if:
$$(g \cdot x)^2 = 0, \quad \forall g \in \{e_1, \dots, e_6, f_1, \dots, f_6\}, \forall x \in P$$

**Economic interpretation:** An economic configuration is stable if the repeated application of a fundamental constraint (rise in interest rates, structural reform, oil shock) does not annihilate it. Frustrated (non-nilpotent) configurations correspond to economic taboos:
* Hyperinflation + collapse in output.
* Deflation + debt explosion.
* Nodal point + saturated frontier (as in China in 2018).

Nilpotence is therefore the condition for systemic stability. It guarantees that the economy cannot remain indefinitely in states of hyperinflation or extreme deflation.

**Proof of the preservation of nilpotence by projection:** Let $x \in P_k^{\text{base}}$ such that $x^2 = 0$. For any generator $g \in \{e_i, f_j\}$, we have:
* If $\{g, x\} = 0$ (anticommutation): $(gx)^2 = gxgx = -g^2 x^2 = 0$.
* If $[g, x] = 0$ (commutation): $(gx)^2 = g^2 x^2 = 0$.

Nilpotence is therefore preserved for the 144 projected pentads. Economic stability is an algebraic property, not an ad hoc assumption.

## Chapter 10 – The Projection Operator $\Pi$: From the 144 Pentads to the 20 Attractors

### 10.1. Definition of the projection operator $\Pi$

Let $\mathcal{H}_P$ be the Hilbert space of the 144 economic pentads. Let $\mathcal{A} = \{A_1, \dots, A_{20}\}$ be the set of the 20 attractors of the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ model.

The projection operator $\Pi$ is a mapping:

$$\Pi : \mathcal{H}_P \longrightarrow \mathcal{A}$$

which associates with each pentad ($P_k^{(g)}$) the attractor ($A_m$) whose triangular face of the Merkabah contains the node corresponding to the pentad.

**Properties of $\Pi$:**

- **Surjectivity:** every attractor is reached by at least one pentad.
- **Non-injectivity:** several pentads (degeneracy) project onto the same attractor.
- **Conservation of polarity:** the polarity signature of the attractor (3P, 2P+1N, 1P+2N, 3N) is determined by the number of positive pentads ($P_k$) and negative pentads ($N_k$) that project onto it.

## Chapter 11 – The Exceptional Network $\Lambda_{72}$ and the Universal Constants $\beta_k$

### 11.1. The $\Lambda_{72}$ lattice as the source of economic resonance frequencies

The $\Lambda_{72}$ lattice, discovered by Gabriele Nebe in 2010, is an even unimodular lattice of dimension 72, with a minimum of 8. It is constructed as a tensor product of the Barnes lattice and the Leech lattice over the ring of integers of the imaginary quadratic field of discriminant -7.

**Properties of the $\Lambda_{72}$ lattice:**

- **Dimension:** $72 = 12 \times 6$, reflecting the $12 \times 12$ structure of the pentads (12 basic pentads $\times$ 12 spectral partitions).
- **Minimal norm:** $\mu = 8$, corresponding to the minimum distance between two stable economic configurations.
- **Unimodularity:** the lattice is even and unimodular (its determinant is 1), which allows it to be interpreted as a genuine pre-geometric configuration space.
- **Automorphisms:** the group of automorphisms of $\Lambda_{72}$ contains $(\mathrm{PSL}_2(7) \times \mathrm{SL}_2(25)):2$, a rich structure capable of encoding the symmetries of economic systems (cycles, regulations, crises).

**Economic interpretation:** $\Lambda_{72}$ is the complete spectrum of resonance frequencies of economic cycles. Each economic attractor (among the 20) corresponds to a linear combination of the 72 vectors of $\Lambda_{72}$. The eigenvalues $\lambda_i$ determine the 'resonance frequency' of the economic state, that is, its sensitivity to shocks and its tendency to shift towards other attractors.

### 11.2. The 15 universal constants $\beta_k$ and their application to economics

De Dominicis (2026) showed that the 48 non-degenerate roots of $\Lambda_{72}$ project onto a 15-dimensional basis, denoted $\{\beta_0, \beta_1, \dots, \beta_{14}\}$. These 15 universal constants are the same ones that govern nuclear masses (physics) and ionisation energies (chemistry).

**Universal spectral formula:**

$$\mathcal{E} = \Lambda_{\text{eco}} \cdot 4^m \cdot \sum_{k=0}^{14} \epsilon_k \beta_k$$

where:

- $\Lambda_{\text{eco}}$ is a scale constant specific to economics (to be determined by historical calibration).
- $m$ is an integer exponent (generally 0, 1 or 2 depending on the degree of polarity).
- $\epsilon_k \in \{-1, 0, 1\}$ is the ternary signature of the attractor.
- $\beta_k$ are the 15 universal constants.

**Economic interpretation:** Each economic attractor is characterised by a vector of 15 coefficients $\epsilon_k$, which encodes its economic 'recipe'. For example:

- Attractor A (extreme perverse growth) activates the first 12 constants $\beta_0$ to $\beta_{11}$.
- Attractor T (terminal depression) activates a restricted combination of $\beta_0, \beta_1, \beta_2$.

### 11.3. The 72 eigenvalues and their economic correspondences

The complete spectrum of economic regimes consists of the 72 eigenvalues of $\Lambda_{72}$.

Among the 72 eigenvalues, 7 critical thresholds ($S_1, \dots, S_7$) play a pivotal role in economic dynamics by triggering transitions between regimes. Their exact values, their degeneracy and the associated macroeconomic policies are detailed in Chapter 13.

## Chapter 12 – The Transition Operator $T$ and Economic Interactions

### 12.1. Definition of the operator $T$ on the space of pentads

Let $\mathcal{H}_P$ be the Hilbert space of the 144 economic pentads. The transition operator $T$ is a linear operator acting on $\mathcal{H}_P$ that generates transformations from one economic configuration to another.

In $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$, $T$ decomposes according to the physical roles of the components of the pentad:

$$T = T_{\text{structure}} + T_{\text{fire}} + T_{\text{water}} + T_{\text{mixed}}$$

**Economic interpretation:**

- $T_{\text{structure}}$: acts on the three bivectors $\{B_1, B_2, B_3\}$, that is, on the production model, the institutions and the rules of the game. An action by $T_{\text{structure}}$ corresponds to a structural reform (e.g. transition from an industrial economy to a service economy, inclusion of GEP in KPIs).
- $T_{\text{fire}}$: acts on the axial element $F = i'v$, that is, on exogenous shocks, innovations and crises. An action of $T_{\text{fire}}$ corresponds to an external event that 'burns' existing structures (e.g. a pandemic, a trade war, an oil shock, a property market crash).
- $T_{\text{water}}$: acts on the polar component $S = 1v$, i.e. on currency, value and capital. An action of $T_{\text{water}}$ corresponds to a monetary or fiscal policy (e.g. quantitative easing, a stimulus package, austerity, tax reform).
- $T_{\text{mixed}}$: combines the three subspaces when the transition involves a simultaneous redistribution (e.g. debt restructuring, corporate mergers).

### 12.2. Angular formulation of the operator $T$

The generators $\{i, j, k, I, J, K\}$ of $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ define a 6-dimensional relational space. The operator $T$ is expressed as an exponential rotation operator:

$$T = \exp \left( i \sum_{a < b} \omega_{ab} L_{ab} \right), \quad L_{ab} = -i \left( \theta_a \frac{\partial}{\partial \theta_b} - \theta_b \frac{\partial}{\partial \theta_a} \right)$$

where $\theta_a$ are angular coordinates associated with the generators, and $\omega_{ab}$ are rotation parameters induced by the economic transition.

**Economic interpretation:** An economic transition is an angular reorientation in configuration space. For example:

- The transition from a rentier economy (attractor H) to a moderate-growth economy (attractor D) corresponds to a rotation in the $(i, j)$ plane (reorientation of the production model).
- The transition from a recessionary economy (attractor I) to a recovering economy (attractor E) corresponds to a rotation in the $(k, I)$ plane (reorientation of monetary policies).

### 12.3. Selection rules

A matrix element of $T$ is non-zero if and only if:

1. **Conservation of the total number of generators:** each bivector contributes 2 generators, whilst the Fire and Water elements contribute 1 each. The sum $\sum N_{\text{gen}}$ is invariant modulo coupling to an external field.
2. **Conservation of generator type modulo gauge:** the spatial generators $\{i, j, k\}$ and the charge generators $\{I, J, K\}$ are individually conserved. The pseudo-scalar $i'$ can only change its projection by crossing the polar thresholds $P_4$ or $N_4$.
3. **Conservation of chirality:** the operator $i'$ projects helicity states. In strong and electromagnetic interactions, $[T, i'] = 0$ (helicity is conserved). In weak (economic) interactions, $T_{\text{fire}}$ induces a $L \leftrightarrow R$ flip via the Ke mode.
4. **Conservation of total angular momentum:** the condition $[L_{ab} + \frac{1}{2} \sigma_{ab}, T] = 0$ ensures exact compensation between intrinsic spin and orbital angular momentum.
5. **Preservation of nilpotence:** $T$ must transform nilpotent states into nilpotent states: $(T|x)^2 = 0$. This condition automatically eliminates divergent self-energy loops and prohibits fusion configurations.

---

- Crossing $S_5$ (Fire) and $S_6$ (Water) triggers intense mixing, releasing the pent-up frustration ($2P+1N \rightarrow 1P+2N$). The system enters a crisis, and only unconventional policies (bailouts, restructuring) can stabilise it.
- Crossing $S_7$ triggers the octave jump, reversing the sign of the effective mass and completing the descent towards $3N$ ($1P+2N \rightarrow 3N$).

### 13.3. The polar thresholds $P_4$ and $N_4$ as public policy operators

The pentads $P_4$ and $N_4$ are the only elements excluded from the tropical belts $C_P$ and $C_N$. Their high degree of connectivity (8 for $P_4$, 9 for $N_4$) makes them topological hinges between the two belts.

**Role of $P_4$:** Authorised transition from expansion to recession (soft landing). In practice, $P_4$ is activated when a government or central bank decides to curb expansion (interest rate rises, fiscal austerity) to prevent overheating.

**Role of $N_4$:** Permitted transition from recession to expansion (controlled recovery). In practice, $N_4$ is activated when a decision is made to stimulate the economy (interest rate cuts, stimulus package) following a recession.

**Golden rule:** Any direct transition between $C_P$ and $C_N$ without passing through $P_4$ or $N_4$ is prohibited. This corresponds to a topological short-circuit (reverse polarity) which causes a systemic collapse.

## Chapter 14 – Retropolarities and Collapses as Violations of Nilpotence

### 14.1. Definition of retropolarity

A retropolarity is a reversal of the economic spectrum: the 3P attractors (expansion) abruptly switch to 3N (depression) without passing through the thresholds. Formally, it is a direct transition $C_P \rightarrow C_N$ (or $C_N \rightarrow C_P$) that violates the threshold rule.

In the spectrum of $\Lambda_{72}$, retropolarity corresponds to a reversal of the sign of the eigenvalues: the $\lambda > 0$ become $\lambda < 0$, and the stable attractors become frustrated configurations.

**Economic interpretation:** A retropolarity is a systemic collapse. It occurs when decision-makers ignore the thresholds $P_4$ and $N_4$ and attempt a direct transition between expansion and recession.

### 14.4. The case of post-2018 China: a system in pre-retropolarity

The diagnosis of the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ model confirms that of the White Paper:

- **Configuration:** $\mathbf{x}_{\text{China}} \approx (0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0)$ → Attractor S (1P+2N).
- **Projected pentads:** $\{P_6, N_5, N_6\}$.
- **Observables:** $\eta < -0.4, d \approx 2, \text{gap} \approx 0.1, R_{\text{threshold}} > 0.7$.

**Interpretation:** The system is in pre-retropolarity. The thresholds $P_4$ and $N_4$ are saturated, and the system is attempting a direct transition $C_N \rightarrow C_P$ without passing through $N_4$. Without intervention (the TRR), this direct transition will trigger retropolarity: hyperinflation, social chaos, the tenth decline.

**Topological solution:** The TRR is a transition controlled by $N_4$. It creates a new pioneering front (ecological regeneration) which enables the system to leave attractor S and reach $C_P$ via a new cycle of Rent Extraction.
