## Chapter 15 - Spectral Observables: \(\eta\), \(d\), gap, \(R_{\text{seuil}}\)

### 15.1. The Discrete Dirac Operator \(D(t)\)

Let \(\Gamma\) be the pentad graph. A local spinor is defined for each pentad \(F\):

$$
\psi_F = \begin{pmatrix} \epsilon_F \\ \phi_F \end{pmatrix}, \quad \epsilon_F \in \{+1, -1\}, \quad \phi_F \in \{0, 1\}
$$

The discrete Dirac operator is a \(24 \times 24\) matrix (12 pentads \(\times\) 2 components) defined by:

$$
(D\psi)_F = \sum_{G \sim F} w_{FG}(t) \sigma_{FG} \psi_G
$$

where:

* \(w_{FG}(t) = e^{-\beta E_{FG}(t)}\) is a coupling weight that depends on the shared frustration energy \(E_{FG}(t)\).
* \(\sigma_{FG}\) is a Pauli matrix.
* \(\beta > 0\) is a rigidity parameter (to be calibrated).

### 15.2. The Four Spectral Observables

**Observable \(\eta(t)\): Spectral Asymmetry**

$$
\eta(t) = \frac{N_{+}(t) - N_{-}(t)}{24}
$$

where \(N_{+}(t)\) is the number of strictly positive eigenvalues, and \(N_{-}(t)\) the number of strictly negative eigenvalues.

* \(\eta > 0\): dominance of the Sheng mode (expansion).
* \(\eta < 0\): dominance of the Ke mode (contraction, recession).
* \(\eta \approx 0\): transition regime (pre-bifurcation).

**Observable \(d(t)\): Effective Spectral Dimension**

$$
d(t) = \frac{2\sum_{k=1}^{24} \lambda_k(t)^2}{\sum_{k=1}^{24} \lambda_k(t)^4} \cdot \frac{\sum_{k=1}^{24} 1}{\sum_{k=1}^{24} \lambda_k(t)^2}
$$

* \(d > 4\): the system is highly connected; shocks propagate rapidly.
* \(d < 2\): the system is fragmented; shocks remain localized.

**Observable \(\text{gap}(t)\): Smallest Positive Spectral Gap**

$$
\text{gap}(t) = \min \{ |\lambda_k(t)| > 0 \}
$$

* \(\text{gap} > 0.5\): robust system.
* \(\text{gap} < 0.2\): fragile system.
* \(\text{gap} \rightarrow 0\): major warning signal.

**Observable \(R_{\text{seuil}}(t)\): Fraction of the Asymmetry at the \(P_4\) and \(N_4\) Thresholds**

$$
R_{\text{seuil}}(t) = \frac{\sum_{k \in \text{seuils}} |\lambda_k(t)|}{\sum_{k=1}^{24} |\lambda_k(t)|}
$$

* \(R_{\text{seuil}} < 0.3\): the system is far from the thresholds.
* \(0.3 \leq R_{\text{seuil}} \leq 0.7\): the system is approaching the thresholds.
* \(R_{\text{seuil}} > 0.7\): red alert — retro-polarization imminent.

### 15.4 — Hierarchy of Observables in Cl(6,6)

#### 15.4.1 — Rationale for the Hierarchical Extension

The four spectral observables defined in §15.2 — $\eta(t)$, $d(t)$, $\mathrm{gap}(t)$, $R_{\mathrm{seuil}}(t)$ — constitute a complete diagnostic system for the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ model. They condense the state of an economic system into four numbers, making it possible to characterize its direction (expansion or recession), its diversity (capacity to propagate shocks), its robustness (resistance to bifurcations), and its tension at the polar thresholds.

However, this monolithic approach presents three structural limitations when moving to Cl(6,6):

**Limitation 1 — Lack of contextual resolution.** The four global observables treat the 144 projected pentads as an undifferentiated block. Yet post-2018 China is not uniformly in Stasis: some spectral sheets (for example, the “Territorial” sheet) are collapsing, while others (for example, the “Cognitive” sheet) are expanding. A global observable conceals these contrasts.

**Limitation 2 — Lack of diagnosis of internal tensions.** The Cl(6,6) model introduces 12 spectral sheets that may be in structural disagreement. An economy may simultaneously be in Sheng mode on one sheet and Ke mode on another. This inter-sheet imbalance is itself a powerful diagnostic signal that the four global observables do not capture.

**Limitation 3 — Lack of fine localization.** The 144 pentads are the elementary building blocks of the system. Identifying which pentads are active, saturated, or in transition makes it possible to establish an “economic acupuncture” type of diagnosis: targeting the precise points where intervention is required to restore circulation.

The hierarchical extension of the observables addresses these three limitations. It transforms the spectral dashboard from an instrument with four needles into a multi-resolution system, analogous to the transition from continental mapping to meridian mapping in traditional Chinese medicine.

---

#### 15.4.3 — Observables per Spectral Sheet (Level 2)

Let $\mathcal{G} = {e_1, \ldots, e_6, f_1, \ldots, f_6}$ be the set of the 12 generators/spectral sheets of Cl(6,6), where:

* ${e_1, \ldots, e_6}$ are the cosmic generators (positive signature, Sheng mode, $\eta > 0$).
* ${f_1, \ldots, f_6}$ are the anti-cosmic generators (negative signature, Ke mode, $\eta < 0$).

For each sheet $g \in \mathcal{G}$, we define a restricted Dirac operator $D_g(t)$ acting on the Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_g$ of the 12 pentads projected onto that sheet. The four local observables are:

\(\eta_g(t) = \frac{n_{P,g}(t) - n_{N,g}(t)}{n_{P,g}(t) + n_{N,g}(t)} \in [-1, +1]\)

where $n_{P,g}(t)$ is the number of active positive (Sheng) pentads on sheet $g$, and $n_{N,g}(t)$ the number of negative (Ke) pentads.

\(d_g(t) = \exp\!\left(-\sum_{k=1}^{12} p_{k,g} \ln p_{k,g}\right), \quad p_{k,g} = \frac{|\lambda_{k,g}|}{\sum_j |\lambda_{j,g}|}\)

\(\mathrm{gap}_g(t) = \min\{|\lambda_{k,g}(t)| > 0\}\)

\(R_{\mathrm{seuil},g}(t) = \frac{|\lambda_{P4,g}| + |\lambda_{N4,g}|}{\sum_{k=1}^{12} |\lambda_{k,g}|}\)

**Economic interpretation.** Each spectral sheet $g$ modulates the intensity and orientation of a basic pentad. For example:

* $P^{(e_2)}_1$: a Keynesian economy ($P_1$) with strong purchasing power ($e_2$) — this is the Trente Glorieuses regime.
* $N^{(f_3)}_5$: a Stasis economy ($N_5$) with profit regulation ($f_3$) — this is the post-2018 Chinese regime on the profit sheet.

The observables by sheet reveal **contextual dissonances**: a system may have $\eta_{e_1} = +0.8$ (innovation in expansion) and $\eta_{f_3} = -0.7$ (finance in contraction), indicating a two-speed economy.

---

#### 15.4.4 — Spectral Threshold Observables (Level 3)

The seven spectral thresholds $S_1, \ldots, S_7$, whose critical values $\sqrt{\lambda_i}$ and economic roles are defined in **Chapter 13 (Table \ref{tab:seuils_spectraux_detail})**, are treated here as binary observables:

\(\sigma_i(t) = \begin{cases} 1 & \text{if } \mathcal{T}(t) \geq \sqrt{\lambda_{S_i}} \\ 0 & \text{otherwise} \end{cases}\)

where $\mathcal{T}(t) = |\nabla \eta \cdot \nabla R_{\mathrm{seuil}}|$ is the topological tension of the system.

The threshold vector $\vec{\sigma}(t) = (\sigma_1, \ldots, \sigma_7) \in {0,1}^7$ encodes the system's threshold-crossing state.

The **threshold vector** $\vec{\sigma}(t) = (\sigma_1, \ldots, \sigma_7) \in {0,1}^7$ encodes the system's threshold-crossing state. Of the 128 possible states, only a few are economically stable:

* $\vec{\sigma} = (0,0,0,0,0,0,0)$: dormant system, below the first threshold.
* $\vec{\sigma} = (1,1,1,1,0,0,0)$: system under moderate tension (case of China 2022–2024).
* $\vec{\sigma} = (1,1,1,1,1,1,1)$: system undergoing an octave jump, cycle change (TRR accelerating).

---

#### 15.4.5 — Pentad Observables (Level 4)

Each of the 144 pentads $P^{(g)}_k$ (with $k \in {1,\ldots,12}$ and $g \in \mathcal{G}$) has four local observables:

**Activity**: $a_{k,g}(t) = |\psi_{k,g}(t)|^2 \in [0,1]$

**Polarity**: $\pi_{k,g}(t) = \mathrm{sgn}(\varepsilon_{k,g}) \in {+1, -1}$

**Connectivity**: $c_{k,g}(t) = \sum_{j \sim k} w_{kj,g}(t)$, number of neighboring pentads coupled on sheet $g$.

**Frustration energy**: $E_{k,g}(t) = |\varphi_{k,g}(t) - \bar{\varphi}_g(t)|^2$, deviation from the mean of sheet $g$.

These observables make it possible to identify **critical pentads**: those with high activity, strong connectivity, and maximum frustration energy. These are the preferred intervention points for the transition operator $T$.

**Application to China.** The spectral diagnosis of post-2018 China (§16.1) identifies the triplet ${P_6, N_5, N_6}$ (configuration $\mathbf{x}_{\text{China}} = (0,0,0,0,1,0)$). The pentad observables reveal:

#### 15.4.6 — Inter-Sheet Relational Observables (Level 5)

Interactions between spectral sheets generate tension observables:

**Asymmetry gap**: $\Delta\eta_{a,b}(t) = |\eta_a(t) - \eta_b(t)|$ for two distinct sheets $a, b \in \mathcal{G}$.

**Inter-sheet correlation**: $C_{a,b}(t) = \mathrm{corr}(\eta_a, \eta_b)$ over a moving window.

**Wuxing tension**: $\mathcal{T}*{\mathrm{Wuxing}}(t) = \max*{a \neq b} \Delta\eta_{a,b}(t)$

The correlation matrix $C(t) \in \mathbb{R}^{12 \times 12}$ is a powerful diagnostic object. A block-diagonal matrix indicates a system fragmented into independent subsets; a matrix with strong coherence indicates an integrated system.

**Wuxing imbalance signature.** The 12 sheets are grouped into 5 families corresponding to the five phases of Wuxing:

The Sheng cycle (Wood → Fire → Earth → Metal → Water → Wood) and the Ke cycle (Wood → Earth → Water → Fire → Metal → Wood) make it possible to diagnose **pathological imbalances**:

* **Sheng imbalance**: $\eta_{\mathrm{Fire}} \gg \eta_{\mathrm{Wood}}$ — expansion without foundation (speculative bubble).
* **Ke imbalance**: $\eta_{\mathrm{Metal}} \ll \eta_{\mathrm{Wood}}$ — excessive regulation stifling innovation.
* **Chinese Stasis**: $\eta_{\mathrm{Earth}} \approx -0.7$, $\eta_{\mathrm{Water}} \approx -0.5$, $\eta_{\mathrm{Fire}} \approx +0.3$ — Earth (profits) and Water (dispersion) are contracting, while Fire (cognitive purchasing power) attempts an immaterial expansion. This is exactly the diagnosis of Part I: the immaterial cannot compensate for the exhaustion of the material.

#### 15.4.7 — Composite Stasis Index

The synthesis of the five levels produces a **composite Stasis Index** defined by:

\(\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stase}}(t) = w_1 |\eta_{\mathrm{total}}| + w_2 \frac{1}{d_{\mathrm{total}}} + w_3 e^{-\alpha \cdot \mathrm{gap}_{\mathrm{total}}} + w_4 R_{\mathrm{seuil},\mathrm{total}} + w_5 \mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{Wuxing}}\)

With $\alpha = 5$, calibrated so that:

* $\mathrm{gap} = 0.1$ (extreme fragility) → $e^{-0.5} \approx 0.61$
* $\mathrm{gap} = 0.2$ (moderate fragility) → $e^{-1.0} \approx 0.37$
* $\mathrm{gap} = 0.5$ (robustness) → $e^{-2.5} \approx 0.08$

\textbf{Note on the gap term}: The term $w_3 , e^{-5 \cdot \mathrm{gap}}$ is a damped exponential form that ensures that the contribution of the gap remains bounded in $[0, 1]$. When $\mathrm{gap} \to 0$ (asphyxiated system), the term tends toward 1 (maximum contribution to Stasis). When $\mathrm{gap} \to 0.5$ (healthy system), the term tends toward $e^{-2.5} \approx 0.08$ (negligible contribution). This form avoids the divergence of the term $1/\mathrm{gap}$, which would make the index unbounded.

The weights $w_i$ are calibrated against the 9 dynastic declines:

The Wuxing imbalance confirms that the Ke cycle (regulation) dominates the Sheng cycle (generation), and that Metal (Rent Extraction) stifles Wood (Inflation).

**Topological prescription.** The TRR (§17.1) is an operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ that must:

1. Desaturate pentad $N_5$ (Stasis node) by activating thresholds $S_5$ (Fire), $S_6$ (Water), $S_7$ (Octave jump).
2. Rebalance Wuxing by restoring the Sheng cycle: **Wood (regulated Inflation) → Fire (Purchasing power) → Earth (Profits/GEP) → Metal (CUR) → Water (Monetary regulation).**
3. Bring $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stase}}$ from 0.64 to < 0.3 by 2032, as the condition for exiting Stasis and restarting a new cycle of material Rent Extraction.

In Chapter 18, this prescription will be included in the spectral dashboard of the 15th Five-Year Plan (2026–2030).

---

### Summary of Part II

This Part II has laid the mathematical foundations for extending the economic model to $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$. We have:

1. **Identified the limitations of the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ model:** closed system, absence of a dynamic origin for attractors and thresholds, absence of a theory of economic 'substance'.
2. **Introduced the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ formalism as a solution:** 12 generators (6 cosmic $e_i$, 6 anti-cosmic $f_j$), Sheng/Ke coupling, 144 pentads as building blocks.
3. **Developed a 5-level hierarchy of observables:**
   - **Level 1:** 4 global observables ($\eta$, $d$, $\mathrm{gap}$, $R_{\mathrm{threshold}}$) for the overall view
   - **Level 2:** 48 observables per spectral sheet for contextual nuances
   - **Level 3:** 7 threshold observables for possible shifts
   - **Level 4:** 144 pentad observables for fine-grained interactions
   - **Level 5:** 66 relational observables for inter-sheet tensions
   
   This hierarchy enables a multi-resolution diagnosis of the economic system, analogous to the four diagnostic methods of traditional Chinese medicine (望闻问切).
4. **Formalised the Stasis composite index** $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}}$, which synthesises the five levels into a single indicator, calibrated against the nine Chinese dynastic declines.
5. **Established cultural correspondences** with the *Yi Jing* (64 hexagrams), the Wuxing (5 phases), TCM (12 meridians), and philosophical traditions (Xiong Shili, Nāgārjuna, Heidegger, Kabbalah).

**Major technical contributions:**

- **Spectral projectors** $\Pi_g$: enable the 12 basic pentads to be projected onto the 12 spectral sheets, generating 144 projected pentads.
- **Dirac operator per sheet** $D_g(t)$: describes the local dynamics of each spectral sheet.
- **Tensor decomposition** $D(t) = \bigoplus_{g=1}^{12} D_g(t)$: shows that the global dynamics is the superposition of 12 local dynamics.
- **Relational observables** $\Delta\eta_{g,h}(t)$ and $C_{g,h}(t)$: measure the structural tensions between spectral sheets.

**Control tools developed:**

- **Multi-level dashboard** (Chapter 18): presents the observables across 5 nested resolutions, enabling a detailed diagnosis of the Chinese Stasis.
- **Advanced visualisations** (Appendix S): heat map of the 12 layers, 5-dimensional radar diagram, inter-layer correlation matrix.
- **Multi-layer control architecture** (Appendix D.12): structures four decision-making layers (Beijing / Province / County / Alert Centre) with the corresponding observables.
- **Complete mathematical formalism** (Appendix O.5): rigorously defines the extended observables and their algorithmic calculation.

**Assessment of China post-2018:**

The $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ model confirms and expands upon the White Paper's analysis:

- **Configuration:** $\mathbf{x}_{\text{China}} \approx (0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0)$ → Attractor S (1P+2N).
- **Projected pentads:** $\{P_6, N_5, N_6\}$ on the dominant leaves $e_3$ (Profits), $f_3$ (Accumulation), $f_6$ (Land).
- **Global observables:** $\eta \approx -0.45$, $d \approx 1.9$, $\mathrm{gap} \approx 0.10$, $R_{\mathrm{threshold}} \approx 0.78$.
- **Composite index:** $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}} \approx 0.64$ → Characterised stasis, close to pre-retropolarity.
- **Wuxing tensions:** $\Delta\eta_{\mathrm{max}} = 1.0$ between Fire and Metal → structural imbalance where Metal (Rent Extraction) stifles Wood (Inflation).

**Interpretation:** The system is in a state of pre-retropolarity. The thresholds $P_4$ and $N_4$ are saturated, and the system is attempting a direct transition from $C_N$ to $C_P$ without passing through $N_4$. Without intervention (the TRR), this direct transition will trigger retropolarity: hyperinflation, social chaos, the tenth decline.

**Outlook:** Part III will apply this formalism to the specific case of the Territorial Regeneration Rent (TRR), demonstrating how it enables a controlled transition via the $N_4$ threshold, activated by a mixed transition operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ combining $T_{\text{structure}}$, $T_{\text{fire}}$ and $T_{\text{water}}$.

# PART III – THE TRR: AN OPERATIONAL SOLUTION

## Introduction to Part III

Parts I and II set out the diagnosis and the mathematical foundations. Part I demonstrated that China reached its Nodal Point in 2018, that the system is locked onto attractor S (dispersion + nodal point), and that the immaterial (AI, carbon, data) is structurally incapable of recreating the mass exogenous demand necessary to emerge from Stasis. Part II formalised this diagnosis within the topological framework of $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$, showing that Stasis is a blockage of transition operators and that the only topologically permitted outcome is a controlled transition via the threshold $N_4$.

This Part III applies this formalism to the specific case of post-2018 China. We diagnose the Chinese Stasis in the language of pentads, formalise the Territorial Regeneration Rent (TRR) as a controlled transition operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ passing through the threshold $N_4$, and propose a spectral dashboard for steering the 15th Five-Year Plan (2026–2030).

This section represents the operational culmination of the demonstration. It shows that the TRR is not merely one ecological policy amongst many, but a concrete application of discrete topology to the regulation of an economic system in crisis, adhering to the laws of the $64 \rightarrow 20$ invariant and offering China a peaceful way out to avoid the tenth dynastic collapse.

---

## Chapter 16 – Diagnosis of the Chinese Stasis in $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$

### 16.1. Projection of the Chinese case onto the network of pentads

The diagnosis of the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ model confirms and refines that of the White Paper: post-2018 China is not merely experiencing a 'Nodal Point', but the terminal exhaustion of a centuries-long trend of material Rent Extraction (initiated in 1978).

For 40 years, China has used mechanisms of spatial avoidance (globalisation, massive investment in infrastructure via LGFVs, urbanisation) to stave off the crisis of overproduction. However, this pioneering front is now saturated (ghost towns, atrophied countryside). At the same time, the mechanism of temporal avoidance (debt) has reached its limits.

As in previous dynastic declines, state control of prices and the structural rigidity of the labour market prevent a natural readjustment of wages and demand. The system is locked onto attractor S (dispersion + nodal point). The Territorial Regeneration Rent (TRR) is therefore not merely a tool for crossing a spectral threshold ($N_4$), but the indispensable mechanism for reorganising the system and initiating a new growth trend, by recreating Value where spatial and temporal avoidance mechanisms have failed.

In the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ model, post-2018 China is projected onto a specific configuration of pentads.

**Economic variables:**

**Projection onto the pentads:** This configuration projects onto a specific triplet of pentads:

$$\mathbf{x}_{\text{China}} \rightarrow \{P_6, N_5, N_6\}$$

- $P_6$ (Domestic consumption economy, active buy-back, concentration): represents the attempt to sustain growth through domestic consumption, but this pentad is 'weakened' as it is no longer supported by a pioneering front.
- $N_5$ (Stasis: nodal point, dispersion, depletion of resources): represents the saturation of the system and the depletion of land value reserves.
- $N_6$ (Domestic austerity: recession, concentration, blocked buy-back): represents austerity and regulatory policies that fail to revive growth.

**Resulting attractor:** The configuration projects onto attractor S (1P+2N): Dispersion and nodal point, systemic fragmentation. This is precisely the diagnosis set out in the White Paper: China is in a state of Stasis.

### 16.2. The blocking of transition operators

In a healthy system, the transition operators ($T_{\text{structure}}, T_{\text{fire}}, T_{\text{water}}$) enable the system to navigate between attractors. But in the Chinese case, these operators are blocked:

- **Blockage of $T_{\text{structure}}$:** The Chinese production model (industrial, based on exports and property) has reached saturation point. Traditional structural reforms (transition to services, innovation) are failing to generate a new cycle of growth because they lack "spatial depth" (they do not create mass employment or regenerate the region).
- **Blockage of $T_{\text{fire}}$:** Exogenous shocks (trade war, COVID, geopolitical tensions) have 'burned' existing structures, but have not created new opportunities. Innovation (AI, carbon, data) suffers from a lack of spatial depth: it is dematerialised and does not generate territorial roots.
- **$T_{\text{water}}$ bottleneck:** Monetary and fiscal policies (interest rate cuts, QE, stimulus) are no longer having any effect. The system is saturated with liquidity, but investment is not picking up because there are no longer any profitable frontiers to explore.
- **Stagnation of $T_{\text{mixed}}$:** Redistribution mechanisms (fiscal, social) are failing to redirect capital flows towards productive sectors. Value accumulates in speculative assets (property, shares) without creating real wealth.

**Consequence:** The system is locked onto attractor S. It cannot return to previous states (towards attractors 2P+1N or 3P) because the frontier sectors are saturated. Nor can it move towards collapse (attractor T) because the Chinese Party-State maintains social cohesion. Stasis is a state of precarious equilibrium, in which the system remains stuck but tensions are building up.

### 16.3. The Nodal Point as a spectral threshold

In the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ formalism, the Nodal Point corresponds to the crossing of several critical spectral thresholds:

However, it has not crossed thresholds $S_5, S_6, S_7$, which correspond to the transition to a new cycle. Stasis is a standstill between $S_4$ and $S_5$: the system is awaiting a trigger that would enable it to cross these thresholds and move into a new regime.

## Chapter 17 – TRR: A Controlled Transition Operator ($T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$)

### 17.1. Formalisation of the TRR as a crossing of the $N_4$ threshold

The White Paper proposes the Territorial Regeneration Rent (TRR) as a solution to the Chinese Stasis. In the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ formalism, the TRR is a threshold-controlled transition at $N_4$ which enables the system to leave the $C_N$ belt (recession) and enter the $C_P$ belt (expansion) via a new cycle of material Rent Extraction.

**Topological mechanism:**

$$\text{Stasis (S)} \xrightarrow[\text{Activation of } N_4]{\text{TRR}} C_P \text{ (New cycle of Rent Extraction)}$$

The threshold $N_4$ is activated by a mixed transition operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ which combines:

- $T_{\text{structure}}$: creation of a new productive model (ecological economy, territorial regeneration).
- $T_{\text{water}}$: redirecting monetary and financial flows towards this new model.
- $T_{\text{fire}}$: using exogenous shocks (climate, environment) as catalysts for the transition.

The TRR is therefore not merely an environmental policy. It is a topological operator that reorients the entire economic system by acting simultaneously on the three components of the pentad.

### 17.2. The three structural reforms as components of $T$

The White Paper identifies three structural reforms for the implementation of the TRR. In the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ formalism, these three reforms are the components of the transition operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$:

**Reform 1: Two Mountains Bank**

- **Component:** $T_{\text{water}}$
- **Mechanism:** Bypassing LGFV (local government financing vehicles) funding through the securitisation of GEP (Gross Ecosystem Product).
- **Topological effect:** Activation of threshold $N_4$ by redirecting capital flows towards ecological regeneration.
- **Interpretation:** The Two Mountains Bank is a monetary operator that creates a new financing channel for ecological frontier initiatives. It enables the $S_6$ threshold (Water Transition) to be crossed by reforming the monetary and financial system.

**Reform 2: Integration of GEP into local executives' KPIs**

- **Component:** $T_{\text{structure}}$
- **Mechanism:** Reform of political incentives; replacement of GDP with GEP as a performance indicator.
- **Topological effect:** Stabilises the transition at $N_4$ by embedding the new production model within institutions.
- **Interpretation:** The integration of GEP into KPIs is an institutional operator that alters the structure of incentives and the rules of the game. It enables the $S_5$ threshold (Fire Transition) to be crossed by creating a new framework for innovation and investment.

**Reform 3: Regenerative Usufruct Certificate (CUR)**

- **Component:** $T_{\text{fire}}$
- **Mechanism:** Reappropriation of rent by local authorities via usufruct certificates on regenerated land.
- **Topological effect:** Anchors the new cycle on $C_P$ by creating a value distribution circuit.
- **Interpretation:** The CUR is a redistributive operator that creates a new Rent Extraction cycle by redirecting value flows towards regenerated territories. It enables the $S_7$ threshold (Octave Leap) to be crossed by triggering a new growth cycle.

## Chapter 18 – Quantification and Management of the TRR through Spectral Observables

### 18.1 — The multi-level spectral dashboard for the 15th Five-Year Plan

#### 18.1.1 — From the monolithic dashboard to the multi-resolution dashboard

The spectral dashboard proposed in the initial version of the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,0)$ model (§18.2, Table 18.1) was based on four global observables — $\eta_{\mathrm{total}}$, $d_{\mathrm{total}}$, $\mathrm{gap}_{\mathrm{total}}$, $R_{\mathrm{threshold},\mathrm{total}}$ — aggregated into a traffic-light system (Green/Amber/Red/Collapse). Whilst this system is sufficient for an overall diagnosis, it masks the contextual dissonances that the extension to $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ brings to light [@DeDominicis_2026_WuXing]: post-2018 China is not uniformly in Stasis; certain spectral sheets (Innovation, Cognitive Purchasing Power) are in Sheng expansion whilst others (Land Profits, LGFV Accumulation) are in deep Ke contraction.

The hierarchical extension of the observables (§15.4, Appendix O.5) enables the construction of a multi-level dashboard that applies the diagnosis across five nested levels of resolution, from the overview (4 needles) to fine-grained interactions (144 pentads). This dashboard is the economic equivalent of the four diagnostic methods of traditional Chinese medicine (望闻问切, *wàng wén wèn qiè*): it takes the pulse of the system at five levels, from the coarsest to the finest.

This paragraph presents the dashboard as applied to the Chinese case of 2026 and how it relates to the management of the 15th Five-Year Plan (2026–2030). The complete mathematical formalism is given in Appendix O.5; the multi-layered decision-making architecture (Beijing / Province / County / Alert Centre) is detailed in Appendix D.12.

#### 18.1.3 — Interpreting the dashboard

**Level 1 — Overview**

The four integrated observables confirm the diagnosis set out in Chapter 16: China is in characterised stasis on the attractor $S$ (dispersion + nodal point), with a triplet of active pentads $\{P_6, N_5, N_6\}$. The composite index $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}} = 0.64$ places the system on the boundary of pre-retropolarity (threshold 0.75). This diagnosis is consistent with the macroeconomic data for 2026: the loss of over 19 million jobs in the construction sector between 2022 and 2026 [@nbs2026migrant], property investment contracting by $-7.9\%$ year-on-year in H1 2026 [@nbs2026h1], and the employment elasticity of the New Productive Forces stabilising at 0.14 [@cmf2026midyear].

**Level 2 — Dominant Patterns**

Level 2 reveals the bipolar signature of the Chinese Stasis: the material sheets ($e_3$ Profits, $f_3$ Accumulation, $f_6$ Land) are in deep Ke contraction ($\eta < -0.5$), whilst the cognitive sheets ($e_1$ Inflation, $e_2$ Purchasing Power) are attempting moderate Sheng expansion ($\eta > +0.2$). This imbalance is the spectral manifestation of the spatial thickness deficit analysed in §6.4: the cognitive rent generated by the SECC (gross margins of 72–86 per cent [@ccid2026computing]) does not trickle down to wages via industrial employment, as the domestic multiplier for data centres (1.2) is structurally lower than that for residential construction (2.5) [@cicc2026h2].

**Level 3 — Thresholds triggered**

The threshold vector $\vec{\sigma} = (1,1,1,1,0,0,0)$ indicates that China has crossed the first four spectral thresholds derived from the $\Lambda_{72}$ network [@Nebe2010] — signalling the exhaustion of the previous growth model — but remains stuck between $S_4$ and $S_5$. The topological tension $T = 0.165$ is close to but below the threshold $S_5 = 0.17195$ (Fire Transition). This blockage is the spectral signature of the absence of a creative exogenous shock: the TRR must provide this trigger by activating $S_5$ (ecological innovation), then $S_6$ (monetary reform via the Two Mountains Bank), then $S_7$ (octave leap towards a new cycle of Rent Extraction).

**Level 4 — Active Pentads**

Level 4 identifies the economic acupuncture points:

- The pentad $N_5^{(f_3)}$ (Stasis with strict profit regulation) is the critical node: maximum activity ($a = 0.78$), strong connectivity ($c = 5$), minimum frustration ($E = 0.08$). It concentrates the dynamics of Stasis and must be desaturated as a priority through the deployment of CURs (§17.2, Reform 3).
- The pentad $P_6^{(e_2)}$ (Domestic consumption with purchasing power) is frustrated ($E = 0.33$): it holds the potential for a consumption-led recovery, but its energy does not flow to the other pentads.
- The pentad $P_1^{(e_1)}$ (Keynesianism with innovation) is in its infancy ($a = 0.35$): it represents the potential of the TRR to create a new material frontier.

**Level 5 — Wuxing Tensions**

Level 5 reveals a structural imbalance in the Wuxing cycle [@Needham1956_WuXing; @granet1922religion]: Metal ($\eta_{\mathrm{Metal}} = -0.70$, Rent Extraction regulation) stifles Wood ($\eta_{\mathrm{Wood}} = -0.05$, innovation), whilst Earth ($\eta_{\mathrm{Earth}} = -0.55$, profits) no longer nourishes Metal. The maximum difference $\Delta\eta_{\mathrm{max}} = 1.0$ between Fire and Metal indicates extreme tension between the cognitive sheets and the financial regulation sheets. The fragmentation index $\mathcal{F} = 0.42$ confirms that the system is bipolar: a Sheng block (Inflation, Purchasing Power) and a Ke block (Profits, Accumulation, Land) are evolving in opposition, with no harmonious flow between the five phases.

Their success determines whether thresholds $S_5$, $S_6$ and $S_7$ are sequentially crossed and whether Stasis is exited.

#### 18.1.5 — Limitations and precautions

The multi-level dashboard does not replace the traditional indicators (GDP, unemployment, inflation) used in the five-year plans; it enriches them with a spectral interpretation. Several precautions are necessary:

- **Provisional calibration:** the weights $w_i$ of the composite index $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}}$ are calibrated against the nine Chinese dynastic declines (Appendix G) [@elvin1973pattern]; their validity for other cultural contexts remains to be tested.
- **Quality of local data:** Level 4 (pentads) and Level 5 (relational) observables require granular data (local GEP, LGFV debt ratios by county [@imf2023china]) which are not always available in real time.
- **The *tang ping* phenomenon:** Stasis is not merely an economic category but an experience lived by millions of young people [@scmp2026tangping; @tangping2026]; the actual unemployment rate amongst 16–24-year-olds (>60 per cent) is an indicator of the erosion of the Mandate of Heaven (§21.1) which spectral observables alone do not capture.
- **Principle of parsimony:** the wealth of 269 elementary observables may tempt decision-makers to multiply interventions; management must focus on the critical observables identified by the Diagnostic layer (Appendix D.12).

#### 18.1.6 — Summary

The multi-level dashboard transforms the management of the 15th Five-Year Plan from a four-handed exercise into a modernised divinatory practice: a dialogue with the system that enables its state to be probed at five levels of resolution, its Wuxing imbalances to be diagnosed, and TRR policies to be adjusted in harmony with the deep laws that structure complex systems. It is the operational translation of the document's central thesis: the economy is a discrete information system (§1.2), and its regulation requires a multi-resolution dashboard, analogous to the four examinations of traditional Chinese medicine.

The following sections (§18.2 to §18.4) detail respectively:
- §18.2: the graphical visualisations of the dashboard (heat map, radar chart, correlation matrix);
- §18.3: the simulation of the transition out of Stasis with and without TRR;
- §18.4: specific management indicators by decision-making level (Beijing / Province / County).

### 18.2 — Graphical visualisations of the spectral dashboard

The multi-level dashboard presented in §18.1 is based on five nested levels of resolution, ranging from the global view (4 integrated observables) to fine-grained interactions (66 relational observables). To make this information actionable for decision-makers, three complementary graphical visualisations are provided. Their full formal specification is given in Appendix S; this section presents their economic interpretation.

#### 18.2.1 — Heat map of the 12 spectral sheets

The heatmap represents the 12 spectral sheets $\mathcal{F} = \{e_1, \ldots, e_6, f_1, \ldots, f_6\}$ according to their local asymmetry $\eta_f(t)$, encoded by a colour gradient:

- **Bright red** ($\eta_f > +0.6$): strong Sheng dominance, contextual expansion.
- **Orange** ($+0.3 < \eta_f \leq +0.6$): moderate Sheng mode.
- **Grey** ($-0.3 \leq \eta_f \leq +0.3$): precarious equilibrium.
- **Light blue** ($-0.6 \leq \eta_f < -0.3$): moderate Ke mode.
- **Dark blue** ($\eta_f < -0.6$): strong Ke dominance, contextual contraction.

**China 2026 Diagnosis (§S.3.3):**

\textbf{Sheet}  & \textbf{Visualisation}  & $\eta$ \\
\hline

$e_2$ Purchasing power  & \textcolor{warning}{██████░░░░}  & $+0.25$ (moderate Sheng) \\
$e_3$ Profits  & \textcolor{blue}{░░░░░░████}  & $-0.70$ (strong Ke) \\

$\eta_{\mathrm{mean}} = -0.45$ \quad $\Delta\eta_{\mathrm{max}} = 1.10$ (Active Rent Extraction $\leftrightarrow$ Innovation)
\end{tcolorbox}

**Economic interpretation.** The chart reveals the bipolar signature of the Chinese Stasis: the material layers ($e_3$ Profits, $e_4$ Buy-back, $e_6$ Land) are undergoing a profound Ke contraction, whilst the cognitive layers ($e_1$ Inflation, $e_2$ Purchasing power) are attempting a moderate Sheng expansion. This imbalance is the spectral manifestation of the deficit in spatial depth analysed in §6.4: the cognitive rent generated by the SECC (gross margins of 72–86 per cent [@ccid2026computing]) is not being channelled into wages via industrial employment [@cmf2026midyear].

#### 18.2.2 — 5-dimensional radar chart

The radar chart superimposes five dimensions normalised to the interval $[0, 1]$:

- The 4 global observables ($\eta_{\mathrm{total}}$, $d_{\mathrm{total}}$, $\mathrm{gap}_{\mathrm{total}}$, $R_{\mathrm{threshold},\mathrm{total}}$).
- The number of activated thresholds $\sum_{i=1}^7 \sigma_i / 7$.

**China 2026 Diagnosis (§S.4.3):**

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=yellow!5!white,colframe=yellow!75!black,title=5-dimensional radar chart — China 2026]
\begin{tabular}{|l|c|c|}
\hline
\textbf{Dimension} & \textbf{Normalised value} & \textbf{Interpretation} \\
\hline
$\eta_{\mathrm{norm}}$ & $0.275$ & Low (Ke dominance) \\
$d_{\mathrm{norm}}$ & $0.158$ & Very low (fragmented system) \\
$\mathrm{gap}_{\mathrm{norm}}$ & $0.10$ & Very low (extreme fragility) \\
$R_{\mathrm{threshold},\mathrm{norm}}$ & $0.78$ & High (critical tension) \\
$\sum \sigma_i / 7$ & $0.571$ & Medium (4 out of 7 thresholds exceeded) \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\vspace{0.3cm}
\textbf{Interpretation:} The radar is \textcolor{failure}{\textbf{unbalanced}} — a characteristic signature of a system in \textbf{pre-retropolarity}.
\end{tcolorbox}

This signature is characteristic of a system in pre-retropolarity: China is a hair's breadth away from systemic collapse. The radar signature allows for an immediate comparison with historical reference regimes (Appendix S, §S.4.4): the Trente Glorieuses (balanced radar), the 1973 oil crisis (contracted radar), Japan in 1990 (collapsed radar), and the Great Depression of 1929 (retropolarity).

#### 18.2.3 — Inter-sheet correlation matrix

The matrix $C(t) \in \mathbb{R}^{12 \times 12}$ encodes the correlations $\mathrm{corr}(\eta_f, \eta_g)$ calculated over a sliding window of 12 quarters (§S.5). Three typical structures can be identified:

- **Block-diagonal matrix:** a system fragmented into independent subsets.
- **High-coherence matrix** ($C_{f,g} \approx +1$): an integrated system.
- **Matrix with bipolar tensions** ($C_{f,g} \approx -1$ between blocks): a system in structural opposition.

**China 2026 Diagnosis (§S.5.3):** the matrix exhibits a bipolar structure:

- **Sheng block** (rows $e_1$, $e_2$): Inflation and Purchasing Power are strongly correlated ($C = 0.70$).
- **Ke Block** (rows $e_3$, $e_4$, $e_6$, $f_2$, $f_3$, $f_4$): anti-cosmic leaves strongly correlated with one another ($C > 0.50$).
- **Sheng$\leftrightarrow$Ke tension:** cosmic and anti-cosmic leaves evolve in opposition ($C < 0$).

The fragmentation index $\mathcal{F} = 1 - \frac{1}{66}\sum_{f<g} |C_{f,g}| = 0.42$ confirms a bipolar system in which two blocks evolve in opposition, without harmonious circulation between the five phases of the Wuxing [@Needham1956_WuXing; @granet1922religion].

### 18.3 — Simulation of the exit from Stasis: scenarios with and without TRR

The simulations presented below project the evolution of spectral observables over the period 2026–2032, according to two contrasting scenarios. They incorporate the Stasis composite index $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}}$ defined in §15.4.7 and §O.5.9:

$$\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}}(t) = w_1 |\eta_{\mathrm{total}}| + w_2 \frac{1}{d_{\mathrm{total}}} + w_3 e^{-5 \cdot \mathrm{gap}_{\mathrm{total}}} + w_4 R_{\mathrm{threshold},\mathrm{total}} + w_5 \mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{Wuxing}}$$

with the weights calibrated against the nine Chinese dynastic declines (Appendix G) [@elvin1973pattern]: $w_1 = 0.23$, $w_2 = 0.18$, $w_3 = 0.23$, $w_4 = 0.21$, $w_5 = 0.15$.

#### 18.3.1 — Scenario 1: Without TRR (continuation of current policies)

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=red!5!white,colframe=red!75!black,title=Scenario 1 — Without TRR (continuation of current policies)]
\begin{tabular}{|c|c|c|c|c|c|l|}
\hline
\textbf{Period}  & $\eta$  & $d$  & $\mathrm{gap}$  & $R_{\mathrm{threshold}}$  & $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}}$  & \textbf{Diagnosis} \\
\hline
2026  & $-0.45$  & $1.9$  & $0.10$  & $0.78$  & $0.64$  & Characterised stasis \\
2028  & $-0.50$  & $1.8$  & $0.08$  & $0.82$  & $0.70$  & Pre-retropolarity \\
2030  & $-0.60$  & $1.2$  & $0.05$  & $0.90$  & $0.75$  & Imminent collapse \\
2032  & $-0.80$  & $0.8$  & $0.02$  & $0.95$  & $0.80$  & \textcolor{failure}{\textbf{Retropolarity (10\textsuperscript{th} dynastic collapse)}} \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\vspace{0.3cm}
\textbf{MULTI-LEVEL DIAGNOSIS IN 2032:}\\
\begin{itemize}
\item Dominant leaf: $e_4$ (Active Rent Extraction) $\rightarrow$ $\eta_{e_4} = -0.85$
\item Critical pentad: $N_5^{(f_3)}$ $\rightarrow$ activity $0.92$, connectivity $6$, frustration $0.02$
\item Wuxing imbalance: $\Delta\eta_{\mathrm{max}} = 1.4$ (Fire$\leftrightarrow$Metal), dominant Ke cycle
\item Threshold vector: $\vec{\sigma} = (1,1,1,1,1,1,1)$ — all thresholds exceeded, octave jump occurred
\item Fragmentation index: $\mathcal{F} = 0.78$ — extreme bipolar system
\end{itemize}
\end{tcolorbox}

**Economic interpretation.** This scenario replicates the trajectory of the nine dynastic declines [@elvin1973pattern]: the depletion of the stock of land value [@rebour2000; @rebour2011], combined with the inability of the intangible sector (SECC) to generate the necessary mass employment (employment elasticity of the New Productive Forces at 0.14 [@cmf2026midyear], and a domestic multiplier for data centres of 1.2 [@cicc2026h2]), leads to a reversal of growth trends. Data from the National Bureau of Statistics confirms this trajectory: the loss of over 19 million jobs in the construction sector between 2022 and 2026 [@nbs2026migrant], property investment contracting by $-7.9\%$ year-on-year in H1 2026 [@nbs2026h1], and a real unemployment rate among 16–24-year-olds exceeding 60 per cent [@scmp2026tangping; @tangping2026]. The phenomenon of *tang ping* — the voluntary withdrawal of 45 per cent of young people from social competition — is the lived manifestation of this degeneration of the attractor $S$ (§7.2).

**Spectral mechanism.** The system remains locked between the thresholds $S_4$ and $S_5$ (§16.3): the topological tension $T = 0.165$ fails to cross $S_5 = 0.17195$ (Fire Transition). Without a creative exogenous shock (TRR), the system attempts a direct transition $C_N \to C_P$ which violates the rule of the polar thresholds $P_4$ and $N_4$ (§13.3), causing retropolarity (§14.1): a sudden inversion of the spectrum, systemic collapse, the tenth dynastic collapse.

#### 18.3.2 — Scenario 2: With TRR (implementation of the 15th Plan)

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5!white,colframe=green!75!black,title=Scenario 2 — With TRR (implementation of the 15\textsuperscript{th} Five-Year Plan)]
\begin{tabular}{|c|c|c|c|c|c|l|}
\hline
\textbf{Period}  & $\eta$  & $d$  & $\mathrm{gap}$  & $R_{\mathrm{threshold}}$  & $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}}$  & \textbf{Diagnosis} \\
\hline
2026  & $-0.45$  & $1.9$  & $0.10$  & $0.78$  & $0.64$  & $N_4$ activation (TRR) \\
2028  & $-0.20$  & $2.8$  & $0.25$  & $0.55$  & $0.48$  & Incipient stasis \\
2030  & $+0.10$  & $3.5$  & $0.35$  & $0.35$  & $0.32$  & Moderate tension \\
2032  & $+0.35$  & $4.2$  & $0.45$  & $0.25$  & $0.22$  & \textcolor{success}{\textbf{Healthy growth}} \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\vspace{0.3cm}
\textbf{MULTI-LEVEL DIAGNOSIS IN 2032:}\\
\begin{itemize}
\item Dominant element: $e_1$ (Inflation) $\rightarrow$ $\eta_{e_1} = +0.60$
\item Active pentad: $P_1^{(e_1)}$ $\rightarrow$ activity $0.72$, Sheng mode, frustration $0.08$
\item Balanced Wuxing: Sheng cycle restored (Wood$\rightarrow$Fire$\rightarrow$Earth$\rightarrow$Metal$\rightarrow$Water)
\item Threshold vector: $\vec{\sigma} = (1,1,1,1,1,1,1)$ — controlled crossing
\item Fragmentation index: $\mathcal{F} = 0.18$ — coherent system
\end{itemize}
\end{tcolorbox}

**Economic interpretation.** The TRR [@rebour2000; @rebour2011] is a transition operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ (§17.1) that combines three structural reforms, each of which activates a specific spectral threshold:

- **Two Mountains Bank** ($T_{\mathrm{water}}$, threshold $S_6$): securitisation of the GEP to circumvent LGFV debt [@imf2023china], reallocation of capital flows towards ecological regeneration. Pilot scheme in Lishui [@ndrc_lishui_pilot_2021; @liangshan_bank_lishui_2021].
- **Integration of GEP into KPIs** ($T_{\mathrm{structure}}$, threshold $S_5$): reform of policy incentives, replacing GDP with GEP as a performance indicator for local officials.
- **Regenerative Usufruct Certificate (CUR)** ($T_{\mathrm{fire}}$, threshold $S_7$): reappropriation of rent by local authorities via usufruct certificates on regenerated land. Roll-out in Xingguo and Ganzhou (§20.1.3).

**Spectral mechanism.** The TRR is a transition controlled by threshold $N_4$, which enables the system to leave the $C_N$ belt (recession) and enter the $C_P$ belt (expansion) via a new cycle of Material Rent Extraction (§17.1). The sequential crossing of the thresholds $S_5$, $S_6$ and $S_7$ corresponds to the deployment timetable of the 15th Five-Year Plan (§17.3, Appendix J).

**Conclusion.** The simulation shows that TRR makes it possible to reverse the trend and emerge from Stasis by gradually crossing the thresholds $S_5$, $S_6$ and $S_7$. Without TRR, the system inevitably shifts towards retropolarity and collapse — replicating the tenth dynastic collapse [@elvin1973pattern]. With TRR, the system reaches a regime of healthy growth ($\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}} < 0.3$) comparable to the Trente Glorieuses (§15.3).

#### 18.4.7 — Summary of indicators

The multi-level dashboard transforms the economic management of a four-pronged exercise into a modernised divinatory practice: a dialogue with the system that enables one to probe its state at five levels of resolution, diagnose its Wuxing imbalances, and adjust TRR policies in harmony with the deep laws that structure complex systems.

The complete architecture of the indicators, their mathematical formalism and their algorithmic implementation are detailed in Appendix O.5 (§O.5.6 to §O.5.10). The integration with the four decision-making levels (Beijing / Province / County / Alert Centre) is presented in Appendix D.12. The graphical visualisations are described in Appendix S (§S.3 to §S.5).

## Chapter 19 – Operational Management of the TRR

### 19.1. Decision-makers' levers of action

Within the framework $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$, decision-makers have four levers of action corresponding to the four components of the transition operator $T$:

**Lever 1: $T_{\text{structure}}$ (Institutional reforms)**

- **Actions:** Incorporating the GEP into KPIs, reforming the planning system, adapting land law.
- **Effect:** Modification of the production model, creation of a new framework for green investment.
- **Target threshold:** $S_5$ (Fire Transition).

**Lever 2: $T_{\text{fire}}$ (Management of exogenous shocks)**

- **Actions:** Using crises (climate, geopolitical tensions) as catalysts; anticipating shocks.
- **Effect:** Transforming constraints into opportunities; accelerating the transition.
- **Target threshold:** $S_5$ (Fire Transition) and $S_7$ (Octave Leap).

**Lever 3: $T_{\text{water}}$ (Monetary and fiscal policies)**

- **Actions:** Establishment of the Two Mountains Bank, securitisation of the GEP, reorientation of credit.
- **Effect:** Redirection of capital flows towards ecological regeneration.
- **Target threshold:** $S_6$ (Water Transition).

**Lever 4: $T_{\text{mixed}}$ (Policy coordination)**

- **Actions:** Implementation of the CUR, coordination between ministries, alignment with existing plans.
- **Effect:** Coupling of the three components, transition controlled by $N_4$.
- **Target threshold:** $N_4$ (negative polar threshold).

### 19.2. The operational timetable

The operational timetable proposed by the White Paper is reinterpreted as a sequence of spectral threshold crossings:

**Phase 1: Pilot (2024–2025)**

- **Objective:** To test TRR mechanisms on a small scale.
- **Actions:** Two Mountains Bank in Lishui; integration of the GEP into local KPIs.
- **Threshold crossed:** $S_5$ (Fire Transition) – activation of ecological innovation.
- **Target observables:** $\eta$ changes from -0.45 to -0.35, $R_{\text{threshold}}$ changes from 0.75 to 0.65.

**Phase 2: Roll-out (2026–2028)**

- **Objective:** To extend the mechanisms to the pilot provinces.
- **Actions:** Widespread adoption of CURs, expansion of the Two Mountains Bank.
- **Threshold crossed:** $S_6$ (Water Transition) – reform of the monetary and financial system.
- **Target observables:** $\eta$ falls from -0.35 to -0.15, $R_{\text{threshold}}$ falls from 0.65 to 0.45.

**Phase 3: Acceleration (2029–2030)**

- **Objective:** Treatment of 5 to 10 million hectares.
- **Actions:** Large-scale roll-out of CURs, massive mobilisation of capital.
- **Threshold crossed:** $S_7$ (Octave jump) – change of cycle, exit from Stasis.
- **Target observables:** $\eta$ changes from -0.15 to +0.10, $R_{\text{threshold}}$ changes from 0.45 to 0.35.

**Phase 4: Widespread Adoption (2031–2035)**

- **Objective:** Full coverage of atrophied rural areas.
- **Actions:** Widespread implementation of the TRR, stabilisation of the new cycle.
- **Threshold crossed:** Stabilisation at $C_P$ (new Rent Extraction cycle).
- **Target observables:** $\eta > +0.30$, $d > 4$, $\mathrm{gap} > 0.4$, $R_{\text{threshold}} < 0.3$.

### 19.3. Risks in the event of inaction or poor implementation

The $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ model identifies three major risks in the event of inaction or poor implementation:

**Risk 1: Continued Stasis**

- **Mechanism:** The system remains locked onto attractor S, with no possible transition.
- **Consequences:** Erosion of the social contract, increased inequality, risk of uprisings.
- **Observables:** $\eta$ remains negative, $R_{\text{threshold}}$ remains greater than 0.7.

**Risk 2: Retropolarity**

- **Mechanism:** An attempt to exit Stasis via a direct transition $C_N \rightarrow C_P$ without passing through $N_4$.
- **Consequences:** Hyperinflation, social chaos, systemic collapse.
- **Observables:** $\eta$ falls sharply, $\mathrm{gap} \rightarrow 0$, $R_{\text{threshold}} \rightarrow 1$.

**Risk 3: Tenth Dynastic Collapse**

- **Mechanism:** A repetition of the fatal cycle of the nine dynastic declines, but on the scale of a modern state.
- **Consequences:** Collapse of the dynasty, political chaos, territorial fragmentation.
- **Observables:** $\eta < -0.8$, $d < 1$, $\mathrm{gap} \approx 0$, $R_{\text{threshold}} \approx 1$.

## Chapter 20 – The Empirical Mapping of TRR

### 20.1. The Empirical Reality: TRR Pilot Zones

The term 'Territorial Regeneration Rent' (TRR) is a theoretical concept. In official Chinese terminology, these mechanisms are classified under the heading 'Mechanisms for Realising the Value of Ecological Products' (生态产品价值实现机制) or 'Two Mountains Banks' (两山银行).

Here is a map of the main areas where the CCP state has already begun to implement the TRR:

#### 20.1.1. The Integrated Epicentre: Lishui Municipality (Zhejiang)

- **Official Chinese name:** 国家生态产品价值实现机制试点 / 丽水"两山银行"
- **Degraded assets being bought back:** Depleted agricultural land, underutilised forests, villages abandoned due to rural exodus.
- **The TRR mechanism:** This is the purest form of the model. The Two Mountains Bank acts as a State-CPC platform that aggregates the usage rights to this degraded land. It then issues loans backed by these assets. The injected credit finances ecological restoration and rural development, creating massive 'advance exogenous demand' in the form of local jobs.

#### 20.1.2. Forest TRR: The cities of Sanming and Nanping (Fujian)

- **Official Chinese name:** 林票制度 / 森林生态银行
- **Degraded assets being repurchased:** Fragmented, unmanaged communal forests with no market value.
- **The TRR mechanism:** Villagers own shares in forests but lack the capital to manage them. State-owned companies buy back the management rights and provide an advance loan to hire local people to replant, maintain and sustainably manage the forest. In exchange, the villagers receive 'Lin-piao' (forestry vouchers), which are negotiable financial instruments.

#### 20.1.3. Water and Soil TRR: Xingguo County and Ganzhou City (Jiangxi)

- **Official Chinese name:** 山水林田湖草生态保护修复工程 / 横向生态补偿
- **Degraded natural assets being restored:** Eroded catchment areas, land contaminated by rare-earth mining, and choked watercourses.
- **The TRR mechanism:** Upstream towns receive funding from the CCP state and 'ecosystem service payments' from downstream towns to decontaminate and restore the soil.

#### 20.1.4. TRR on a Macro-Scale: Hainan Province

- **Official Chinese name:** 国家生态文明试验区 / GEP核算体系
- **Degraded assets redeemed:** The entire island territory, threatened by coastal property speculation.
- **The TRR mechanism:** South Island has banned speculative property development. The "GEP" (the green equivalent of GDP) is used as an indicator to assess local officials and as collateral to secure massive bank loans.

#### 20.1.5. The Transitional TRR (Material Carbon): The city of Huzhou (Zhejiang) and Chongyi County (Jiangxi)

- **Official Chinese name:** 竹林碳汇 / 林业碳票
- **Degraded wealth repurchased:** Ageing, unharvested bamboo groves.
- **The TRR mechanism:** This acts as a bridge between the physical TRR and the Third Intangible Transformation. The CCP state funds the physical maintenance of bamboo forests by trading the carbon credits generated on the Chinese domestic market.

### 20.2. The 'photovoltaic+' model: a large-scale proto-TRR

Over the past decade, China has developed an original model of territorial regeneration based on the synergy between renewable energy production, ecological regeneration and rural development. This model, known as 'photovoltaic+' (光伏+), rests on three complementary pillars:

**First pillar: an income-generating economic infrastructure**

The solar power station forms the cornerstone of the scheme. By reducing water evaporation by 20 to 30 per cent and halving wind speeds, the panels create a microclimate conducive to the regrowth of vegetation in areas where it could no longer survive. In Talatan, in the Gobi Desert in Qinghai, an area that was once 98.5 per cent desertified has seen vegetation recover beneath the panels, with vegetation cover reaching 80 per cent. Annual grass production there stands at 118,000 tonnes, enough to feed nearly 100,000 sheep.

**Second pillar: profitable agricultural or livestock activities**

The space beneath and between the panels is not left unused. The so-called 'photovoltaic sheep' (光伏羊) model has been systematically implemented in Qinghai: ewes graze beneath the panels, eating the grass (which reduces the power stations' maintenance costs) and their manure fertilises the soil.

**Third pillar: anchoring external demand in the local area**

Whereas AI and data concentrate value in a few major cities, the 'photovoltaic+' model creates mass local employment. Wages paid for construction, maintenance and operation are spent locally, triggering a regional multiplier effect. This is precisely what the intangible sector lacks to lift China out of Stasis.

## Chapter 21 – The TRR and the Preservation of the Mandate of Heaven

### 21.1. The Mandate of Heaven as an implicit social contract

Within the framework of the morphogenetic process, the Mandate of Heaven is not a cosmological abstraction. It is an implicit social contract between the CCP State and the population: the CCP State ensures prosperity, stability and social harmony; the population grants legitimacy to the CCP State.

If the CCP State no longer fulfils its part of the bargain (declining prosperity, mass unemployment, growing inequalities), the Mandate erodes and the costs of coercion rise. The alternative paths threaten this contract in three ways:

- **Polarised growth 2.0:** No mass job creation → youth unemployment, inequality, *tang ping*. The CCP state loses its ability to deliver shared prosperity.
- **Neo-feudal withdrawal:** Abandonment of the peripheries → territorial divisions, rural uprisings, collapse of virtuous legitimacy.
- **Authoritarian ecological planning:** Temporary public sector jobs, economic inefficiency, corruption → unpopularity.

### 21.2. The TRR as a restoration of political virtue

The TRR is the only path that tangibly repairs the damage caused by the previous cycle of accumulation: ghost towns are repurposed, withered countryside is regenerated, and groundwater levels are restored. In doing so, it restores the sovereign's political virtue (德, *De*) — the legitimacy based on the ability to ensure harmony between Heaven, Earth and Mankind.

### 21.3. TRR as a response to the demographic crisis

Falling birth rates and an ageing population are reducing the available labour force. The TRR addresses this challenge through:

- **Mass rural employment:** young people can find productive work in revitalised rural areas (agrivoltaics, aquaculture, ecotourism), without having to migrate to overcrowded cities.
- **The attractiveness of rural areas:** vibrant countryside, with services, schools and jobs, encourages higher birth rates and slows the exodus.
- **Reduced living costs:** ecological regeneration (water, soil, energy) lowers the cost of living in rural areas.

---

## Chapter 22 — The geopolitical impact of TRR: from spatial avoidance to the diplomacy of regeneration

### 22.1. Introduction — Geopolitics as an external projection of the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ topology

The preceding chapters have established that post-2018 China is locked onto the attractor $S$ (dispersion + nodal point), with a composite Stasis index $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}} = 0.64$ (§18.1.3). The Territorial Regeneration Rent (TRR) has been formalised as the transition operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$, enabling a transition controlled by the threshold $N_4$ (§17.1). However, this analysis has remained strictly internal: it treats China as a closed system.

However, the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ topology does not stop at borders. The formalism of the 12 spectral sheets and the 144 projected pentads can be extended to the international arena: every trading partner, every host state for Chinese investment, and every geopolitical bloc can be modelled as a spectral sheet projected onto the Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}$ of the world-system. Geopolitics thus becomes the dynamics of inter-sheet couplings, governed by the same laws of nilpotence and the same spectral thresholds as the internal dynamics.

This chapter elaborates on this extension. It shows that the TRR is not merely a domestic policy of ecological regeneration: it is the condition of possibility for a complete overhaul of China's geopolitical stance, marking the end of external spatial avoidance and the advent of a 'diplomacy of regeneration'. This diplomacy offers the Global South an alternative model to Western imperialism — no longer the extraction of rent, but the co-regeneration of Value.

### 22.2. Theoretical framework: TRR as a way out of the spatial avoidance paradigm

#### 22.2.1. Recap: spatial avoidance in the Theory of Rent Extraction (§2.2 and §5.5)

The Theory of Rent Extraction (ToRE) has established that growth is based on the transfer of assets from the Space of Value to the Space of Wealth, financed by advance credit. This process has an absolute limit: the Nodal Point, reached when the stock of readily mobilisable Value is exhausted (§1.6). To postpone this deadline, economic systems have two avoidance mechanisms at their disposal:

- **Spatial avoidance:** geographical expansion (conquests, colonies, frontier expansion) or export to new markets.
- **Temporal avoidance:** postponing the crisis through debt, fiat currency and financialisation.

Imperial Chinese history has illustrated these two mechanisms (Appendix G): the Han, Tang, Yuan and Qing dynasties successively employed spatial avoidance (conquests, the Silk Road, westward expansion); the Song, Southern Jin and Ming dynasties employed temporal avoidance (fiat currency, inflows of silver, credit). Post-1978 China has replicated this duality on a continental scale: internal spatial avoidance (urbanisation, mega-infrastructure, Tudi Caizheng) followed by external spatial avoidance (globalisation, the Belt and Road Initiative, productive investments abroad) and temporal avoidance (LGFV debt, SECC).

#### 22.2.2. The simultaneous exhaustion of both forms of avoidance in 2018

The spectral analysis in Chapter 16 established that China reached its terminal Nodal Point around 2018:

- **Internal spatial avoidance saturated:** urbanisation rate > 65 per cent, land with high externalities fully absorbed, mega-infrastructure projects in the West reaching their limits of profitability (§5.1).
- **External spatial avoidance saturated:** productive investments abroad (HPAL factories in Indonesia, projects in Venezuela) come up against the Sovereignty Rent of host states (§5.5.3). Indonesian Decree No. 144 (2026) demonstrated the structural vulnerability of these assets [@esdm2026decree144].
- **Saturated temporal avoidance:** LGFV debt at 60,000 billion yuan (48 per cent of GDP), with the SECC generating external rents without channelling them into mass employment (§6.3.3).

The threshold vector $\vec{\sigma} = (1,1,1,1,0,0,0)$ (§18.1.3) indicates that China has crossed the first four thresholds (exhaustion of the previous model) but remains stuck between $S_4$ and $S_5$. The avoidance mechanisms are structurally exhausted.

#### 22.2.3. TRR as a qualitative substitute for quantitative expansion

TRR brings about a fundamental epistemological break: it substitutes qualitative regeneration (recreating Value where Value had been destroyed) for quantitative expansion (constantly acquiring more virgin land, conquering ever more territories).

Formally, TRR is a $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ operator (§17.1) that combines:

- $T_{\mathrm{structure}}$: the creation of a new productive model underpinned by territorial ecology.
- $T_{\mathrm{water}}$: reorientation of financial flows (Two Mountains Bank, securitisation of the GEP).
- $T_{\mathrm{fire}}$: use of the climate crisis as a catalyst (Regenerative Usufruct Certificates).

This combination complies with the conservation laws of ToRE: it does not create Value *ex nihilo*, but regenerates degraded Value. It enables the controlled crossing of the $N_4$ threshold, avoiding retropolarity (systemic collapse) which penalises any direct transition $C_N \rightarrow C_P$ (§13.3).

**Geopolitical projection:** If the TRR is a transition operator for China, it can be projected onto international partners. Every country in the Global South stuck in its own Nodal Point can import the operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ to trigger its own cycle of ecological Rent Extraction. Chinese geopolitics ceases to be a logic of spatial avoidance (exporting overcapacity, conquering markets) and becomes a logic of co-regeneration (exporting a model of value creation).

### 22.3. TRR as a substitute for Western economic imperialism

#### 22.3.1. Limitations of the Western model: financialisation and retropolarity

The West has temporarily 'hijacked' the fatal cycle of the Nodal Point (§3.1–3.3) through two mechanisms:

- **Spatial imperialism:** colonies, natural resources, and foreign markets to absorb overproduction.
- **Temporal avoidance:** financialisation, money creation through private credit, and speculative bubbles.

But having exhausted its physical resources (colonisation completed around 1914) and its financial resources (the 2008 crisis revealing the impossibility of maintaining fictitious bubbles indefinitely), the West is sinking into a topological impasse similar to that of China. Its response is massive monetisation (quantitative easing, dilutive inflation) and an attempt at a direct transition from $C_N$ to $C_P$ through the injection of liquidity — which, according to the $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ model, violates nilpotence and paves the way for global systemic retropolarity (§14.2).

#### 22.3.2. The TRR as an endogenous recreation of Value (vs. extraction)

TRR proposes a radically different model. Instead of extracting Value (from colonies, mineral resources, financial rent), it regenerates Value. Instead of transferring Wealth from the peripheries to the centre (substitution of externalities, §1.5), it creates Wealth where it had been destroyed.

Formally, TRR complies with the law of conservation of ToRE: it does not create value *ex nihilo*, but transforms degraded value (impoverished soils, under-utilised forests, eroded catchment areas) into regenerated value (restored ecosystems, securitised GEPs, rent-generating CURs). This transformation constitutes a new cycle of Rent Extraction, but one based on qualitative regeneration rather than quantitative expansion.

#### 22.3.3. Comparative table: Western imperialism vs. diplomacy of regeneration

The following table compares the two models for exiting the Nodal Point:

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Geopolitical comparison of strategies for exiting the Nodal Point}
\label{tab:geopolitical_comparison_nodal_exit}
\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{3.2cm}  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X @{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Dimension}  & \textbf{Western model (Financialisation)}  & \textbf{Post-2018 China (SECC/Enclaves)}  & \textbf{Post-TRR China ($T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$)} \\
\midrule
\textbf{Topological mechanism}  & Forced direct transition $C_N \rightarrow C_P$ (violation of nilpotence)  & External spatial avoidance (Fortified Port Enclaves) + temporal avoidance (SECC)  & Transition controlled by the $N_4$ threshold \\
\addlinespace

\addlinespace

\addlinespace

\addlinespace

**Interpretation:** Both the Western model and the post-2018 Chinese model (SECC/Enclaves) are attempts to avoid the Nodal Point. The former violates nilpotence (direct transition $C_N \rightarrow C_P$), whilst the latter saturates the avoidance mechanisms (closed outer pioneer front, SECC without spatial thickness). Only the post-TRR model complies with topological laws: it activates the operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$, which enables a transition controlled by $N_4$, creating a new underlying asset (the Regenerative Value) and restoring the Rent Extraction cycle.

### 22.4. Redefinition of the New Silk Roads (BRI)

#### 22.4.1. Diagnosis: relative failure of productive investment

The Belt and Road Initiative (BRI), launched in 2013, was conceived as a mechanism for external spatial avoidance: to export China's industrial overcapacity to the countries of the Global South, secure flows of raw materials, and create new markets. However, the results have been mixed (§5.4):

- **Transport corridors** (gas pipelines, railways, maritime routes): a relative success, as they are underpinned by guaranteed flows and are immune to Sovereign Rent.
- **Local productive investments** (HPAL plants in Indonesia, projects in Venezuela): a structural failure, as they are vulnerable to the 'sovereign rent' of the host states. Indonesian Decree No. 144 (2026) demonstrated that a sovereign state can unilaterally change the rules of the game, rendering the Chinese financial arrangement untenable [@esdm2026decree144; @scmp2026indonesia].
- **Fortified Port Enclaves** (Chancay, Piraeus, Hambantota): a success, as they are designed to shield investments from sovereign rent (§5.5.5).

The lesson is clear: the BRI cannot serve as a new material frontier for China. Productive investment abroad does not generate the 'exogenous demand lead' needed to reignite the Chinese engine. The economic returns are too low and too slow to offset the sudden halt of the domestic property-driven engine [@hurley2019docking].

#### 22.4.2. The model of Fortified Port Enclaves: a geopolitical turning point, not a new cycle

The port of Chancay (Peru), inaugurated in November 2024, is the archetype of Fortified Port Enclaves (§5.5.5). Unlike HPAL plants, Chancay is a purely extractive infrastructure: a mineral port designed to export South American copper, lithium and iron ore to China, with no local processing capacity whatsoever.

The contracts include structural conditions that constrain the host state's sovereign manoeuvre: exclusive operating rights granted to COSCO, regulatory exemptions secured through legislation, and an all-assets pledge securing all the project's assets. This structure makes any unilateral change to the rules by the host state economically destructive.

But Chancay is not a new cycle of 'Rent Extraction'. It is a geopolitical merry-go-round: it secures the flow of raw materials, but does not create value in China. It does not generate mass employment, does not restore the Sheng cycle, nor does it cross the $N_4$ threshold. It is a defensive response to the exhaustion of the material frontier, not a solution to Stasis.

#### 22.4.3. Exporting the TRR model: the BRI as 'Two Mountains diplomacy'

The TRR offers a strategic alternative: instead of exporting an extraction model (logistical colonisation), China can export a model of topological regeneration. Developing countries, which are themselves stuck at their Nodal Point (exhaustion of arable land, ecological degradation, unsustainable debt), can import the operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ to trigger their own cycle of ecological Rent Extraction.

Formally, the projection of the operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ onto the spectral sheets of partner countries is written as:

$$T_{\mathrm{mixed}}^{(\mathrm{partner})} = T_{\mathrm{structure}}^{(\mathrm{partner})} + T_{\mathrm{fire}}^{(\mathrm{partner})} + T_{\mathrm{water}}^{(\mathrm{partner})}$$

where each component is tailored to the local context:

- $T_{\mathrm{structure}}^{(\mathrm{partner})}$: local institutional reform (integration of GEP into national KPIs, land law reform).
- $T_{\mathrm{fire}}^{(\mathrm{partner})}$: use of exogenous shocks (climate crisis, ecological degradation) as catalysts.
- $T_{\mathrm{water}}^{(\mathrm{partner})}$: reorientation of financial flows (securitisation of ecological assets, creation of local green banks).

This projection complies with the conservation laws of the ToRE: it does not create Value *ex nihilo*; it regenerates locally degraded Value. It enables the controlled crossing of the $N_4$ threshold for the partner country, avoiding retropolarity (systemic collapse) which penalises any attempt at direct transition.

#### 22.4.4. Regional case studies

**South-East Asia (Indonesia, Laos)**

Indonesia activated its Sovereign Rent via Decree No. 144 (2026), rendering the financial arrangements for the Chinese HPAL plants in Sulawesi unsustainable [@esdm2026decree144]. China was forced to withdraw, leaving Indonesia with unfinished infrastructure and unpaid debts, whilst bearing part of the cost itself (§5.5.2).

Beijing has learnt its lesson: future investments must be designed to be more resilient to the host country's Sovereign Rent. The TRR offers an alternative: instead of exporting vulnerable factories, China can export the Two Mountains Bank model, helping Indonesia to securitise its ecological assets (forests, mangroves, coral reefs) to finance their regeneration. This approach respects Indonesian sovereignty (no full collateral, no regulatory exemptions) whilst creating value locally.

Laos, faced with the collapse of its external debt and the degradation of its forests (deforestation for rubber plantations), can adopt the Regenerative Usufruct Certificates (CUR) model: local communities receive financial securities backed by forest regeneration, thereby financing their own development without relying on vulnerable Chinese investment.

**Africa (Madagascar, Angola, Tanzania)**

Sub-Saharan Africa is the testing ground for regeneration diplomacy. China has rolled out a comprehensive agricultural investment strategy there (§5.5.4): Madagascar (technology transfer, hybrid rice), Angola (130,000 hectares of soya and maize), Tanzania (32,000 hectares), Zambia (99-year leases), DRC (2.8 million hectares of palm oil).

These operations have low externalities (§5.5.4): they secure supply chains, but do not create a cycle of buy-back in China. TRR offers an alternative: instead of leasing land for export, China can help African countries regenerate their own soils (combating desertification, agroforestry, watershed restoration), thereby creating value locally and reducing dependence on Chinese imports.

The Two Mountains Bank model can be adapted to the African context: securitisation of ecological assets (forests, soil, water) to finance regeneration, the creation of mass rural employment, and a reduction in rural-to-urban migration. This approach respects African sovereignty (no 99-year leases, no full collateral) whilst creating value locally.

**Latin America (Peru–Chancay, Venezuela)**

Peru illustrates the dual nature of Chinese investment: Chancay (a fortified port enclave, a success story) versus agricultural and mining projects (vulnerable to 'sovereign rent'). The TRR offers an alternative: rather than concentrating investment on mining (copper, lithium), China can help Peru regenerate its Andean ecosystems (restoring high-altitude pastures, combating soil erosion, protecting water sources), thereby creating value locally and reducing dependence on raw material exports.

Venezuela provides a textbook example: Chinese loans secured against oil (over $50 billion between 2007 and 2015) came up against institutional collapse, triggering the Sovereign Rent not by decree, but through the state's bankruptcy [@hurley2019docking]. China was forced to accept losses and debt rescheduling. The TRR offers an alternative: instead of lending against oil (a vulnerable, underground asset), China can help Venezuela securitise its ecological assets (Amazonian forests, biodiversity, water resources), thereby creating value locally and reducing dependence on oil.

**Russia (49-year agricultural leases)**

Russia is the most successful example of Chinese agricultural outsourcing: over 670,000 hectares leased (estimated at 1 million hectares), 49-year contracts, rent of 300 to 600 yuan per hectare, and a requirement to employ at least 30 per cent Russian labour (§5.5.4). These leases represent a niche accumulation: they secure supply flows (soya, wheat, maize, meat), but do not create a cycle of buy-back in China.

The TRR offers an alternative: instead of leasing land for export, China can export the Two Mountains Bank model, helping Russia to securitise its ecological assets (boreal forests, black soil, water resources) to finance their regeneration. This approach respects Russian sovereignty (no 49-year leases, no full collateral) whilst creating value locally.

### 22.5. Sino-American rivalry: two models for exiting the Nodal Point

#### 22.5.1. The West: forced transition $C_N \rightarrow C_P$ (violation of nilpotence)

Since the 1970s, the Western economy has been in a topological impasse similar to that of China, but has responded with financialisation (fictitious bubbles) and massive monetisation (dilutive inflation). The $\mathrm{Cl}(6,6)$ model predicts that this attempt at a direct transition $C_N \rightarrow C_P$ (without passing through the polar thresholds $P_4$ or $N_4$) violates nilpotence and paves the way for global systemic retropolarity (§14.2).

The spectral observables for the West in 2026 confirm this diagnosis:

- $\eta_{\mathrm{West}} \approx -0.3$: moderate recession, Ke dominance.
- $d_{\mathrm{West}} \approx 2.5$: moderate fragmentation.
- $\mathrm{gap}_{\mathrm{West}} \approx 0.15$: high fragility.
- $R_{\mathrm{threshold,West}} \approx 0.65$: high tension, close to the red alert threshold (0.7).

The West is attempting to force the transition through the injection of liquidity (quantitative easing, negative interest rates, debt monetisation), but this approach violates the principle of nilpotence: it does not create Value; it dilutes existing Wealth. The model predicts a reversal of polarity by 2030–2035: systemic collapse, social chaos, territorial fragmentation.

#### 22.5.2. China: transition controlled by $N_4$ via the TRR

China, through the TRR, activates the operator $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$, which complies with the law of conservation by creating a new underlying asset (regenerative value). The controlled crossing of the $N_4$ threshold enables the system to leave the recession belt ($C_N$) and enter the expansion belt ($C_P$) via a new cycle of Material Rent Extraction (§17.1).

China's spectral observables in 2032 (scenario with TRR, §18.3.2) confirm this trajectory:

- $\eta_{\mathrm{China}} = +0.35$: moderate expansion, Sheng dominance.
- $d_{\mathrm{China}} = 4.2$: harmony and diversity restored.
- $\mathrm{gap}_{\mathrm{China}} = 0.45$: system robustness.
- $R_{\mathrm{threshold,China}} = 0.25$: exit from the red zone.
- $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis,China}} = 0.22$: healthy growth.

#### 22.5.3. The asymmetric advantage of endogenous regeneration

The TRR offers China a decisive asymmetric advantage in the Sino-American rivalry:

- **Compliance with nilpotence:** China complies with topological laws (transition controlled by $N_4$), whilst the West violates them (direct transition $C_N \rightarrow C_P$).
- **Endogenous creation of value:** China creates value locally (ecological regeneration), whilst the West dilutes existing wealth (monetisation of debt).
- **Mass employment:** the TRR generates mass rural employment (agrivoltaics, aquaculture, ecotourism), whilst Western financialisation destroys industrial jobs.
- **Political legitimacy:** the TRR restores the Mandate of Heaven (harmony between Heaven, Earth and humankind), whilst Western financialisation erodes the legitimacy of institutions.

This asymmetric advantage is neither military nor technological: it is topological. China masters the topologically authorised trajectory, whilst the West is sinking into a forbidden trajectory (retropolarity).

#### 22.5.4. The SECC as a temporary complement

The Computing Capacity Export Strategy (SECC, §6.3.3) is not at odds with the TRR: it serves as a temporary complement to it. The SECC generates considerable external rents (gross margins of 72–86 per cent for Chinese hyperscalers) which underpin trade surpluses and the hyperscalers' balance sheets in the short term. However, it does nothing to address the deficit in internal spatial depth: with an employment elasticity of 0.14 and a domestic multiplier of 1.2 [@cmf2026midyear; @cicc2026h2], the intangible sector remains structurally incapable of recreating the mass employment needed to replace land-based finance.

The SECC acts as a palliative buffer: it makes it possible to artificially maintain social stability in the absence of new drivers of growth (§6.4.4). However, it cannot replace the TRR as the main engine for emerging from Stasis.
