\section*{Appendix S – Advanced spectral dashboard for Cl(6,6)}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Appendix S – Advanced spectral dashboard for Cl(6,6)}

### S.1 — Introduction and rationale

#### S.1.1 — Aim of the appendix

The four spectral observables defined in Chapter 15 — $\eta(t)$, $d(t)$, $\mathrm{gap}(t)$, $R_{\mathrm{threshold}}(t)$ — constitute a comprehensive diagnostic system for the Cl(6,0) model. They summarise the state of an economic system in four figures, enabling its direction, diversity, robustness and tension at the polar thresholds to be characterised. Appendix D demonstrated how these observables feed into a management application designed for decision-makers.

However, the extension to Cl(6,6) introduces a structural richness that the four global observables do not capture:

- **12 spectral sheets** (6 cosmic $e_i$, 6 anti-cosmic $f_j$) modulate the intensity and orientation of each pentad.
- **144 projected pentads** constitute the building blocks of the system.
- **7 spectral thresholds** $S_1, \ldots, S_7$ control the transitions between regimes.
- **66 inter-sheet relationships** encode the structural tensions between perspectives.

Appendix S presents the formal and operational tools for harnessing this richness. It complements Appendix D (basic control) and Appendix O (mathematical formalism) by providing:

1. The **formal definitions** of the extended observables.
2. Appropriate **visualisations** (heat map, radar chart, correlation matrix).
3. The **Stase composite index** $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stase}}$ and its calibration.
4. A **comprehensive calculation algorithm** for implementation.
5. **Calibrated historical examples** to validate the model.

#### S.1.3 — Guiding Principles


The advanced dashboard adheres to three principles:

**Principle 1 — Multi-resolution.** The decision-maker must be able to zoom from the global level (4 needles) to the detailed level (144 pentads), just as a Chinese physician moves from the general pulse (脉) to the examination of a specific meridian (经气).

**Principle 2 — Cultural coherence.** The extended observables must respect the correspondences with Chinese correlative thought (Wuxing, TCM, Yi Jing) established in Chapter 24.

**Principle 3 — Falsifiability.** Each observable must be calculable from concrete economic data, and the model must be testable retrospectively against the 64 historical regimes in Appendix M.

---

### S.2 — Formal definitions of extended observables

#### S.2.1 — Notation

Let $\mathcal{G} = \{e_1, \ldots, e_6, f_1, \ldots, f_6\}$ be the set of 12 generators/spectral leaves of Cl(6,6), where:

- $\{e_1, \ldots, e_6\}$ are the cosmic generators (Sheng mode, $\eta > 0$).
- $\{f_1, \ldots, f_6\}$ are the anti-cosmic generators (Ke mode, $\eta < 0$).

Let $\mathcal{P} = \{P_1, \ldots, P_6, N_1, \ldots, N_6\}$ be the set of the 12 basic pentads.

A **projected pentad** is written as $P_k^{(g)} = g \cdot P_k$, where $g \in \mathcal{G}$ and $k \in \{1, \ldots, 12\}$. There are $12 \times 12 = 144$ projected pentads.

For each leaf $g \in \mathcal{G}$, we define the restricted Dirac operator $D_g(t)$ acting on the Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_g$ of the 12 pentads projected onto that leaf.

#### S.2.2 — Level 2 Observables: by spectral sheet

For each sheet $g \in \mathcal{G}$, the four local observables are given by:

**Local asymmetry**:
$$\eta_g(t) = \frac{n_{P,g}(t) - n_{N,g}(t)}{n_{P,g}(t) + n_{N,g}(t)} \in [-1, +1]$$
where $n_{P,g}(t)$ is the number of active positive pentads (Sheng) on the sheet $g$, and $n_{N,g}(t)$ is the number of active negative pentads (Ke).

**Local spectral dimension**:
$$d_g(t) = \exp\!\left(-\sum_{k=1}^{12} p_{k,g} \ln p_{k,g}\right), \quad p_{k,g} = \frac{|\lambda_{k,g}|}{\sum_j |\lambda_{j,g}|}$$

**Local spectral gap**:
$$\mathrm{gap}_g(t) = \min\{|\lambda_{k,g}(t)| > 0\}$$

**Local threshold fraction**:
$$R_{\mathrm{threshold},g}(t) = \frac{|\lambda_{P4,g}| + |\lambda_{N4,g}|}{\sum_{k=1}^{12} |\lambda_{k,g}|}$$

#### S.2.3 — Level 3 Observables: spectral thresholds

The seven spectral thresholds are binary or continuous observables:

$$\sigma_i(t) = \begin{cases} 1 & \text{if } T(t) \geq \sqrt{\lambda_{S_i}} \\ 0 & \text{otherwise} \end{cases}$$

where $T(t) = \|\nabla \eta \cdot \nabla R_{\mathrm{threshold}}\|$ is the topological tension of the system.

The values of the seven spectral thresholds $S_1, \ldots, S_7$ and their economic signals are given in the **Table in Chapter 13**. 

The **threshold vector** $\vec{\sigma}(t) = (\sigma_1, \ldots, \sigma_7) \in \{0,1\}^7$ encodes the system’s crossing state.

#### S.2.4 — Level 4 Observables: pentads

For each projected pentad $P_k^{(g)}$ (where $k \in \{1,\ldots,12\}$ and $g \in \mathcal{G}$), we define four local observables:

**Activity**: $a_{k,g}(t) = \|\psi_{k,g}(t)\|^2 \in [0,1]$

**Polarity**: $\pi_{k,g}(t) = \mathrm{sgn}(\varepsilon_{k,g}) \in \{+1, -1\}$

**Connectivity**: $c_{k,g}(t) = \sum_{j \sim k} w_{kj,g}(t)$

**Frustration energy**: $E_{k,g}(t) = |\varphi_{k,g}(t) - \bar{\varphi}_g(t)|^2$

#### S.2.5 — Level 5 Observables: Inter-leaf Relational Observables

For two distinct leaves $a, b \in \mathcal{G}$:

**Asymmetry gap**: $\Delta\eta_{a,b}(t) = |\eta_a(t) - \eta_b(t)|$

**Inter-leaf correlation**: $C_{a,b}(t) = \mathrm{corr}(\eta_a, \eta_b)$ over a sliding window.

**Wuxing tension**: $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{Wuxing}}(t) = \max_{a \neq b} \Delta\eta_{a,b}(t)$

The correlation matrix $C(t) \in \mathbb{R}^{12 \times 12}$ is a powerful diagnostic tool.

### S.3 — Heat map of the 12 spectral bands

#### S.3.1 — Visualisation principle

The heatmap represents the 12 spectral sheets as a $1 \times 12$ matrix, where each cell is coloured according to the value of $\eta_g(t)$:

- **Bright red** ($\eta_g > +0.6$): strong Sheng dominance (expansion).
- **Orange** ($+0.3 < \eta_g \leq +0.6$): moderate Sheng mode.
- **Grey** ($-0.3 \leq \eta_g \leq +0.3$): precarious equilibrium.
- **Light blue** ($-0.6 \leq \eta_g < -0.3$): moderate Ke mode.
- **Dark blue** ($\eta_g < -0.6$): strong Ke dominance (contraction).

### S.4 — 5-dimensional radar diagram

#### S.4.1 — Principle

The radar diagram superimposes five dimensions on a single graph:

- The **4 global observables** ($\eta_{\mathrm{total}}$, $d_{\mathrm{total}}$, $\mathrm{gap}_{\mathrm{total}}$, $R_{\mathrm{threshold},\mathrm{total}}$).
- The **7 triggered thresholds** (represented by their sum $\sum \sigma_i$).

Each axis is normalised to $[0, 1]$ to allow for superposition.

\addlinespace

\bottomrule
\end{tabular}

### S.5 — Inter-sheet correlation matrix

#### S.5.1 — Definition

The correlation matrix $C(t) \in \mathbb{R}^{12 \times 12}$ is defined by:

$$C_{a,b}(t) = \mathrm{corr}(\eta_a, \eta_b) = \frac{\mathrm{cov}(\eta_a, \eta_b)}{\sigma_{\eta_a} \sigma_{\eta_b}}$$

calculated over a sliding window of 12 quarters, for two distinct sheets $a, b \in \mathcal{G}$.

#### S.5.2 — Interpretation

- $C_{a,b} \approx +1$: leaves $a$ and $b$ move in phase (structural coherence).
- $C_{a,b} \approx 0$: the leaves are independent (fragmentation).
- $C_{a,b} \approx -1$: the leaves move in opposition (structural tension).

#### S.5.3 — Example: China 2026

- **Sheng block** (lines 1–2): Innovation and Purchasing Power are strongly correlated ($C = 0.7$).
- **Ke block** (lines 3–6, 8–12): the anti-cosmic leaves are strongly correlated with one another ($C > 0.5$).
- **Sheng↔Ke Tension**: the cosmic and anti-cosmic leaves are in opposition ($C < 0$). This is the hallmark of a **bipolar** system in which the two modes are in conflict.

#### S.5.4 — Fragmentation index

The **fragmentation index** is defined as:

$$\mathcal{F}(t) = 1 - \frac{1}{66} \sum_{a < b} |C_{a,b}(t)|$$

- $\mathcal{F} \approx 0$: a highly coherent system (all leaves evolve together).
- $\mathcal{F} \approx 1$: a highly fragmented system (the leaves are independent).

**China 2026**: $\mathcal{F} = 0.42$ — moderate fragmentation, with a clearly distinct Sheng bloc and a Ke bloc.

---

### S.6 — Stasis Composite Index

#### S.6.1 — Definition

The Stase Composite Index summarises the five levels of the hierarchy into a single number:

$$\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}}(t) = w_1 |\eta_{\mathrm{total}}| + w_2 \frac{1}{d_{\mathrm{total}}} + w_3 \, e^{-5 \cdot \mathrm{gap}_{\mathrm{total}}} + w_4 R_{\mathrm{threshold},\mathrm{total}} + w_5 \mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{Wuxing}}$$

\textbf{Note}: The term $w_3 \, e^{-5 \cdot \mathrm{gap}}$ ensures that the index is bounded. See §15.4.7 for a detailed justification.

\subsubsection*{S.6.2 --- Calibration of weights}

The weights $w_i$ are calibrated against the nine Chinese dynastic declines 
(see \textbf{Table \ref{tab:ponderation_indice_stase_O}}, Appendix O, §O.5.9.2).

\subsubsection*{S.6.3 --- Thresholds for the composite index}

The thresholds for the composite index are defined in 
\textbf{Table \ref{tab:thresholds_stasis_index_O}} (Appendix O, §O.5.9.3).

\subsubsection*{S.6.4 --- Calculation for China 2026}

\begin{equation}
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}} = 0.23 \times 0.45 + 0.18 \times \frac{1}{2.1} + 0.23 \times e^{-5 \times 0.12} + 0.21 \times 0.78 + 0.15 \times 1.1 = 0.644
\end{equation}

\textbf{Diagnosis}: China is in a state of \textbf{characterised stasis} ($0.5 < \mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}} < 0.7$).

#### S.7.2 — Economic interpretation

**Level 1**: The system is generally contracting ($\eta < 0$), fragmented ($d < 2$), fragile ($\mathrm{gap} < 0.2$), and under critical strain ($R_{\mathrm{threshold}} > 0.7$). The composite index confirms the characterised Stasis.

**Level 2**: The material layers (Profits, Accumulation, Land) are in deep contraction, whilst the cognitive layer (Innovation) is attempting an expansion that fails to compensate for the deficit in spatial depth (§6.4).

**Level 3**: The system has crossed the first four thresholds (exhaustion of the previous model) but remains stuck between $S_4$ and $S_5$. It awaits a trigger to cross $S_5$ (Fire Transition).

**Level 4**: The pentad $N_5$ (Stasis) is the critical node: it is the most active and the most connected. Any intervention must desaturate it.

**Level 5**: The Wuxing imbalance confirms that the Ke cycle (regulation) dominates the Sheng cycle (generation). Metal (Rent Extraction) stifles Wood (Inflation).

#### S.7.3 — Topological prescription

The TRR (§17.1) is a $T_{\mathrm{mixed}}$ operator which must:

1. **Desaturate the pentad $N_5$** by activating the thresholds $S_5$ (Fire), $S_6$ (Water), $S_7$ (Octave leap).
2. **Rebalance the Wuxing** by restoring the Sheng cycle: Wood (ecological innovation) → Fire (green jobs) → Earth (territorial GEP) → Metal (CUR) → Water (regeneration of catchment areas).
3. **Reduce $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}}$ from 0.64 to < 0.3** by 2032, a prerequisite for emerging from Stasis and launching a new cycle of material rent extraction.

### S.8 — Complete calculation algorithm

#### S.8.1 — Inputs

The input data required to calculate the extended observables are 
listed in \textbf{Table \ref{tab:input_data_observables_O}} 
(Appendix O, §O.5.10.1).

\subsection*{S.8.2 Complete calculation algorithm}

The algorithm for calculating the extended observables and the computational complexity table 
are presented in detail in \textbf{Appendix O, §O.5.10}. 
The full calculation of the 701 elementary observables takes less than one 
second on a standard computer (complexity $O(22\,615)$), enabling 
real-time control.

**Note:** §15.4.2 mentions **269 elementary observables**, which corresponds to 4 + 48 + 7 + 144 + 66 = 269, counting 1 observable per pentad.
It should be noted that 269 is the number of *independent* observables and 701 is the *total* number (including the 4 observables per pentad).

#### S.9.2 — The dashboard as a TCM practitioner

The advanced dashboard is the modern equivalent of an **imperial court physician**:

1. **Inspection (望)**: the heat map of the 12 leaves reveals the distribution of qi within the economic body.
2. **Listening (闻)**: the correlation matrix reveals the harmonies and dissonances between the meridians.
3. **Inquiry (问)**: the activated thresholds reveal which energy gates are open or closed.
4. **Palpation (切)**: the active pentads reveal the acupuncture points to be stimulated.

The synthetic diagnosis ($\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stase}}$) is equivalent to the **health check-up** carried out by the doctor following the four examinations.

### S.11 — Limitations and caveats

#### S.11.1 — Limitations of the model

1. **Provisional calibration**: The weights $w_i$ of the composite index are calibrated against the nine Chinese dynastic declines. Their validity for other cultural contexts remains to be tested.

2. **Imperfect data**: Level 4 (pentads) and Level 5 (relational) observables require detailed data that is not always available.

3. **Sliding window**: Inter-period correlations are calculated over a 12-quarter window. This choice is arbitrary and may influence the results.

4. **Non-linearities**: The model assumes linear relationships between the observables. Non-linearities (threshold effects, bifurcations) are not fully captured.

#### S.11.2 — Precautions for use

1. **The dashboard is a decision-support tool, not an automated system.** The observables are composite indicators; they are no substitute for detailed analysis.

2. **The composite index is a signal, not a prediction.** A $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}} > 0.7$ indicates a risk, not a certainty of collapse.

3. **The parallels with Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) are heuristic.** They provide a framework for interpretation, not a scientific validation of the model.

4. **The model must be falsified.** If the observables consistently fail to anticipate historical tipping points, the model must be revised.

#### S.11.3 — Future work

1. **Refinement of the calibration** using longer historical time series (the Roman Empire, medieval Europe).

2. **Development of a predictive simulator** incorporating the extended observables to test macroeconomic policy scenarios.

3. **Extension to other complex systems** (climate, ecosystems, social networks) to test the universality of the 64→20 invariant.

---

### S.12 — Summary

Appendix S presented the formal and operational tools for exploiting the structural richness of Cl(6,6):

1. **Hierarchy of observables**: 5 levels, 701 elementary observables, of which a significant subset (~20) is sufficient for operational control.

2. **Appropriate visualisations**: heat map of the 12 leaves, 5-dimensional radar chart, inter-leaf correlation matrix.

3. **Composite Stasis Index**: $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Stasis}}$ synthesises the 5 levels into a single number, calibrated against the 9 dynastic declines.

4. **Multi-level dashboard**: presents the observables across five nested levels, enabling a detailed diagnosis of Chinese Stasis.

5. **Complete calculation algorithm**: enables real-time implementation, with a computational complexity of $O(22615)$.

6. **Correlations with TCM**: the dashboard is the modern equivalent of the four diagnostic methods of traditional Chinese medicine.

7. **Historical validation**: the calibrated examples (the Trente Glorieuses, the oil crisis, Japan, the Republic of China) confirm the model’s validity.

The advanced spectral dashboard transforms economic management from a four-needle exercise into a **modernised divinatory practice**: a dialogue with the system that enables us to probe its state, diagnose its imbalances, and adjust policies in harmony with the profound laws that structure complex systems.

This is the mathematisation of Chinese structural wisdom: the I Ching, the Five Elements and Traditional Chinese Medicine are no longer poetic metaphors, but formal frameworks for which the Cl(6,6) formalism provides a rigorous mathematical translation.

---

# Appendix T – Beyond the 144 Pentads: Open Prolongations

\subsection*{T.1 Position of the Problem}

The current model rests on the chain:

$$
\text{Cl}(6,0) \xrightarrow{12 \text{ pentads}} \times 12 \text{ generators of Cl}(6,6) \xrightarrow{144 \text{ pentads}} \xrightarrow{\Pi} 20 \text{ attractors}
$$

This construction yields **144 pentads** to describe economic dynamics. Applied to an economy of **1.4 billion agents** (China), this represents a granularity of approximately **10 million people per pentad**. This is manifestly insufficient to capture regional, sectoral, and social heterogeneities.

**Question**: can the model be extended to a finer granularity while preserving the 64 → 20 invariant?

\subsection*{T.2 Three Axes of Prolongation}

### Axis A — Projection onto the Bivectors of Cl(6,6)

**Construction**: instead of projecting the 12 pentads onto the 12 generators (vectors) of Cl(6,6), project them onto the **66 bivectors** $\Lambda^2(\mathbb{R}^{6,6})$.

**Granularity**: $12 \times 66 = 792$ pentads (× 5.5 relative to 144).

**Presumed advantage**:
- Bivectors encode **relations** between directions, not only directions.
- They capture **interactions** between spectral sheets (level 5 of the multi-level architecture).
- They allow modelling of economic **couplings** (sector × region, for example).

**Difficulty**: 66 is not 64. Two bivectors must be identified and excluded (likely the analogues of the scalar and pseudoscalar), by imposing a purity or nilpotence constraint.

**If the constraint works**: one obtains $12 \times 64 = 768$ pentads, and the 64 → 20 invariant remains applicable.

### Axis B — Projection onto the Trivectors of Cl(6,6)

**Construction**: project onto the **trivectors** $\Lambda^3(\mathbb{R}^{6,6})$, of dimension $\binom{12}{3} = 220$.

**Granularity**: $12 \times 220 = 2640$ pentads (× 18 relative to 144).

**Presumed advantage**:
- Trivectors are the analogue of the **20 trivectors of Cl(6,0)** which define the 20 attractors.
- They capture **ternary structures** (triplets of spectral sheets).
- They are naturally linked to the **holomorphic 3-forms** $\Omega$ and $\bar{\Omega}$ of the SU(3)×SU(3) context.

**Difficulty**: 220 is not a simple multiple of 64. One must either restrict (by purity, nilpotence), or accept a change of invariant.

### Axis C — SU(3)×SU(3) Structure and Pure Spinors

**Construction**: use the **compatible pair of pure spinors** $(\Phi_+, \Phi_-)$ of Cl(6,6) to define a subspace of dimension 6 (the intersection of annihilators).

**Granularity**: depends on the decomposition of the Cl(6,6) spinor (dimension 64) under SU(3)×SU(3).

**Presumed advantage**:
- This is the **richest** structure and the best established in the literature (generalized complex geometry).
- It captures the **internal symmetries** of the economy (the 8+8 generators of SU(3)×SU(3)).
- It allows a **natural embedding** of the 20 attractors as 20 pure 3-forms.

**Difficulty**: the "20" does not appear as a cardinal, but as a **representation dimension**. The exact decomposition must be verified.

\subsection*{T.3 Comparative Table of Prolongations}

| Axis | Projection object | Number | Granularity | Link with 64 → 20 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Current** | 12 generators (vectors) | 144 | 10 M pers./pentad | Direct |
| **A** | 66 bivectors | 792 | 1.8 M pers./pentad | After exclusion of 2 |
| **B** | 220 trivectors | 2640 | 530 k pers./pentad | To be reformulated |
| **C** | Pure spinors SU(3)×SU(3) | Variable | Continuous | Structural |

\subsection*{T.4 Presumed Advantages over the 144 Pentads}

### T.4.1 Spatial Resolution

With 144 pentads, China is described at the scale of **~10 million people**. This masks:
- regional disparities (coastal vs interior),
- sectoral disparities (industry vs services),
- social disparities (urban vs rural).

With 792 (Axis A), one descends to **~1.8 million** — the scale of an average Chinese **prefecture**.
With 2640 (Axis B), one descends to **~530,000** — the scale of a Chinese **county**.

### T.4.2 Relational Richness

- **Vector-pentads** (144) describe **states**.
- **Bivector-pentads** (792) describe **relations** between states.
- **Trivector-pentads** (2640) describe **three-body configurations**.

This is a qualitative leap: from **photography** to **kinematics**, then to **dynamics**.

### T.4.3 Coherence with the Multi-Level Architecture

Recall the current architecture:

| Level | Type | Number |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Global | 4 |
| 2 | Per sheet | 48 |
| 3 | Spectral threshold | 7 |
| 4 | Pentad | 144 |
| 5 | Relational (inter-sheet) | 66 |

**Level 5 (66) corresponds exactly to the bivectors.** This suggests that Axis A **unifies** levels 4 and 5: bivector-pentads are simultaneously pentads (level 4) and relations (level 5).

### T.4.4 Preservation of the Invariant

If Axis A works (66 → 64 by exclusion of 2 bivectors), then:

$$
12 \times 64 = 768 \xrightarrow{\Pi} 20 \text{ attractors}
$$

The **64 → 20 invariant is preserved**, and granularity is multiplied by 5.33.

This is the **best compromise** between richness and coherence.

\subsection*{T.5 Prolongations Beyond}

If Axis A is validated, one may envisage:

### Prolongation A1 — Iteration

Apply the same projection to the 768 pentads:

$$
768 \xrightarrow{\Pi} 20 \text{ attractors} \xrightarrow{\text{projection}} \dots
$$

Each attractor could be **unfolded** into sub-attractors, yielding a **fractal** structure.

### Prolongation A2 — Combination with SU(3)×SU(3)

Cross the 768 pentads with the 16 generators of SU(3)×SU(3):

$$
768 \times 16 = 12288 \text{ pentads}
$$

Granularity: **~114,000 people/pentad** — the scale of an **urban neighbourhood** or a **large village**.

### Prolongation A3 — Spinorial Structure

Use the complete Cl(6,6) spinor (dimension 64) as a **phase space**:

$$
768 \times 64 = 49152 \text{ pentads}
$$

Granularity: **~28,000 people/pentad** — the scale of a **local community**.

\subsection*{T.6 Difficulties and Limits}

### T.6.1 Mathematical Difficulty

The passage 66 → 64 requires a **precise constraint** (purity, nilpotence, preservation of the annihilator). This constraint must be:
- **natural** (not ad hoc),
- **demonstrable** (explicit computation),
- **coherent** with the 64 → 20 invariant.

### T.6.2 Empirical Difficulty

With 792 or 2640 pentads, one needs **as much data** to calibrate the model. This is a collection and processing challenge.

### T.6.3 Interpretive Difficulty

Each pentad must have a **clear economic meaning**. Multiplying pentads without multiplying interpretations leads to **sterile complexity**.

\subsection*{T.7 Synthesis: Which Strategy to Adopt?}

### Three-Step Recommendation

**Step 1 — Validate Axis A (bivectors)**

- Explicitly compute the 66 bivectors of Cl(6,6).
- Identify the 2 bivectors to exclude (analogues of scalar/pseudoscalar).
- Verify that $12 \times 64 = 768$ pentads project correctly onto 20 attractors.

**Step 2 — Test Coherence with SU(3)×SU(3)**

- Decompose the Cl(6,6) spinor under SU(3)×SU(3).
- Verify whether a component of dimension 20 (or 64) appears.
- Embed the 20 attractors as 20 pure 3-forms.

**Step 3 — Envisage Prolongations**

- If Axis A works, explore Axes B and C.
- If Axis A fails, fall back on Axis C (SU(3)×SU(3) structure), which is better established in the literature.

\subsection*{T.8 Conclusion}

The **144 pentads** constitute a **valid first step** but are insufficient to describe an economy of 1.4 billion agents. The envisageable prolongations are:

1. **Axis A (bivectors)**: 792 pentads, × 5.5, preserves the invariant after exclusion of 2 bivectors.
2. **Axis B (trivectors)**: 2640 pentads, × 18, changes the invariant.
3. **Axis C (SU(3)×SU(3))**: continuous structure, better founded in the literature.

**Axis A is the most promising**: it preserves the 64 → 20 invariant, unifies levels 4 and 5 of the architecture, and offers granularity compatible with the Chinese prefectural scale.

**Axis C is the most theoretically solid**: it relies on generalized complex geometry, an established mathematical domain.

**The optimal strategy**: validate Axis A, then cross it with Axis C to obtain a structure that is both **rich** (fine granularity) and **rigorous** (founded in the literature).

These prolongations are left open. Motivated researchers will find here:
- the **precise questions** to address,
- the **explicit computations** to perform,
- the **numerical tests** to run,
- the **references** to consult (Rowlands, generalized complex geometry, pure spinors).

**End of Document**
